.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:4:source: Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/convergence-gate.md @ HEAD 26e6e55e70f90715e3300552cace4ae6e13e737b
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:11:name: convergence-gate
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:12:description: Adoption gate — new patterns/repos/SDKs require convergence evidence (≥3 T1 sources + ≥2 named T2 practitioners + ≥3 months stability OR strong-provenance-express predicate), else REJECT-until-convergence
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:18:# TIER-3 port provenance only: ported from Z:/claude/rules/convergence-gate.md HEAD. Parent CCC retains cycle-N audit evidence; claude-sota authority is the TIER-1/TIER-2 sources cited inside this rule.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:21:A new pattern, repo, SDK, or external primitive is ADOPT-NOW only when CONVERGENCE evidence is present. Convergence means the pattern exists independently in multiple authoritative places, not just one. Absent convergence, the default verdict is **REJECT-until-convergence** — not STUDY-PILOT, not "maybe later."
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:23:This gate stacks ABOVE the `.claude/skills/sota-research/SKILL.md` 5-row LOAD-BEARING bar (LOCAL — sota-research skill present at `.claude/skills/sota-research/SKILL.md`). It is stricter: the 5-row bar allows STUDY-PILOT on any `[UNKNOWN]`; the convergence gate does not — any missing convergence axis is a REJECT until that axis is satisfied OR the STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS predicate fires.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:29:| **Independent T1 sources** | ≥3 distinct orgs/repos/papers implementing the pattern | file:line cites in 3 different `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/` dirs OR 3 different official docs URLs — not 3 pages of the same source |
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:33:Any axis failing = REJECT-until-convergence. Verdict line must name the specific missing axis + the exact evidence that would satisfy it.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:47:1. **List the 3 T1 sources explicitly**. If you cannot name 3 different `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/file:line` OR 3 different official docs URLs where the pattern appears independently, the axis fails.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:50:4. **If ANY axis fails**, write `REJECT-until-convergence` as the verdict, with a bullet list specifying exactly what evidence would close each missing axis.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:51:5. **Only then** run the sota-research 5-row bar. The convergence gate is a precondition — passing it doesn't auto-ADOPT, but failing it auto-REJECTS.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:57:### Axis 1 — Independent T1 sources (≥3 distinct)
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:68:| `mcp__context7__resolve-library-id` + `query-docs` | Official docs as T1 (when adopting a library) | ACTIVE |
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:69:| `mcp__exa__web_search_exa` (category:research paper) | Peer-reviewed papers + named-org blogs as T1 | ACTIVE |
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:87:3. Require URL + date on each citation per the base rule
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:89:**Bidirectional**: the README-grep step upgrades verdicts when collaborator-metadata missed a signal AND confirms-REJECT when README also shows zero. Without the README-grep step, canonical repos with direct practitioner endorsements can be scored [UNKNOWN] → REJECT incorrectly.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:111:| **STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS** (5th band, predicate-gated): `age ≥ 30d` AND axis-1 = official-org maintainership AND axis-2 = named-T2 endorsement OR maintainer-org IS T2-equivalent | **Firm axis-3 PASS — relaxed maturity gate** | When provenance is strong (org-level T1) AND a named-T2 OR org-equivalent endorsement exists, the launch-spike risk that motivates the strict 90-day gate is dominated by maintainer-org commitment. Examples of org-level T1 patterns: vercel-labs / anthropics / openai / getzep / langchain / microsoft / google / facebook-research / google-deepmind / openai-evals / equivalents. The LAUNCH-SPIKE band still applies for `unknown-org + age<90d + cpd>50` — protection holds for the truly anti-pattern cases (solo-org launch with non-permissive license = REJECT-UNTIL-CONVERGENCE unchanged) |
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:117:No single tool is authoritative. Running any ONE of the above in isolation produces narrow-grep false-negatives (single-tool signal ≠ convergence). The convergence check requires ≥3 distinct tool-types returning compatible evidence — otherwise treat the finding as "one-tool signal," which is not convergence.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:121:## Anti-pattern: retroactive convergence manufacturing
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:123:Do not construct convergence evidence after choosing the verdict. The sequence must be: (1) collect convergence evidence → (2) count axes → (3) verdict. Not (1) pick verdict → (2) find supporting cites.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:125:If a candidate looks promising but fails convergence today, the right move is to **queue a re-audit date** (90 days out, or when a named T2 practitioner is spotted adopting it), not lower the gate.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:129:When running the 5-row bar's Row 2 (Numeric — measurable improvement), a README presenting **≥3 numeric performance claims without methodology citation** (no repro script link, no benchmark repo, no dataset name) is **auto-FAIL** — do not attempt live A/B to verify, just REJECT. This short-circuits the primary-source rule: vendor marketing numbers that aren't backed by a reproducible artifact are false-by-default until proven otherwise.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:134:3. If ≥3 claims lack citations, Row-2 auto-FAIL → REJECT verdict bypasses expensive verification step
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:135:4. If 1-2 claims lack citations, run the live-verify anyway (lower signal-to-noise)
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:153:If a README opens with a `[!CAUTION] Scam alert` block warning of impostor domains distributing malware, that's a STRONG axis-2 self-flag — the upstream itself is signaling the candidate exists in a high-volume scam-attractor space (typically a viral-young repo where bad actors create lookalike forks). Auto-REJECT axis-2 even before counting unsourced numeric claims.
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:155:**How to apply**: when scanning a README for axis-2 named-practitioner endorsements, also scan the FIRST 10 lines for `Scam alert` / `[!CAUTION]` blocks warning of impostor domains. If present, the candidate has been or currently is in a high-attention attractor space — REJECT-until-convergence even if other axes look strong (the self-flag indicates ongoing trust-volatility).
.claude/rules\convergence-gate.md:157:**Distinguishes from §Anti-pattern Row-2 fabrication-test FAIL**: that rule fires on candidate AUTHORS publishing unsourced claims (deception via omission). This rule fires on candidate AUTHORS warning about THIRD-PARTY impostors (deception attractor signal). Same auto-REJECT axis-2 verdict, different mechanism.

.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:9:# Reference: constituents=[TIER-1-DIRECT @ Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/agent-skills/skills/deprecation-and-migration/SKILL.md:10-12 @742dca5, TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION @ claude-sota-installed adaptation]; effective_tier=TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION per citation-discipline.md rule #8 [VERIFIED 2026-05-08 via Wave 82m-B sota-researcher Top-3 CITE-IMPORT #2 + Wave 82x codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION fix-forward F-001 cite-class reframe] — addyosmani/agent-skills (33,500 stars MIT, named-author Addy Osmani / Google Chrome team). Upstream verbatim at SKILL.md:10-12: "Code is a liability, not an asset. Every line of code has ongoing maintenance cost — bugs to fix, dependencies to update, security patches to apply, and new engineers to onboard. Deprecation is the discipline of removing code that no longer earns its keep, and migration is the process of moving users safely from the old to the new."
.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:32:This is why deprecation requires **active migration**, not just announcement. Users can't "just switch" when they depend on behaviors the replacement doesn't replicate.
.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:40:3. **Does a replacement exist?** → If no, build the replacement FIRST. Never deprecate without an alternative. **Replacement readiness gate** (per Wave 82x codex T1 fix-forward F-003 / upstream SKILL.md:71-75): replacement must (a) cover critical use cases of the old system, (b) have documentation + migration guides, AND (c) be proven in production (not just "theoretically better") before deprecation starts.
.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:51:**Default to advisory.** Use compulsory only when maintenance cost or risk justifies forcing migration. Compulsory deprecation requires migration tooling, documentation, and support; a deadline alone is not sufficient. Per Wave 82x codex T1 fix-forward F-002: upstream Osmani SKILL.md:62-65 mandates default-to-advisory + compulsory-with-tooling guardrail.
.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:58:4. **Removal**: delete old code, dependencies, docs, references — verify ZERO active usage via metrics/logs/dependency analysis (NOT just `rg "<old-symbol>"` text search; per Wave 82x codex T1 fix-forward F-005 / upstream SKILL.md:112-118,201-206); then remove tests + docs + config + deprecation notices
.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:62:**If you own the surface being deprecated, you own user migration or backward-compatible updates; do not leave consumers to discover the path themselves.** Per Wave 82x codex T1 fix-forward F-004 / upstream SKILL.md:106. This makes the owner responsibility EXPLICIT — distinct from the "Announce-and-leave" anti-pattern below (which describes the failure mode this rule prevents).
.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:67:- **Hard-remove-without-replacement**: deprecating before a viable migration path exists. Refuted by §The deprecation decision question 3.
.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:69:- **Speculative-deprecation**: marking code `@deprecated` because "we might replace it someday". This violates kiss-dry-yagni — only deprecate when a real replacement exists. Sister to "speculative ADD" anti-pattern.
.claude/rules\deprecation-discipline.md:76:- `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/research-protocol.md` 3 gates — RECALL → INVESTIGATE → VERIFY before deprecating any surface (verify replacement exists + dependents migrated)

.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:25:Single-model design produces silent drift. Opus 4.7 drafting a rule looks coherent to Opus reading it back; codex GPT-5.5 reading the same rule independently catches mismatches Opus's self-audit misses (cite line-range errors, terminology drift across sibling rules, internal-vs-external state conflation, narrative-vs-protocol confusion). The cross-model consensus contract is: **Opus 4.7 designs + drafts → GPT-5.5 codex audits at lifecycle touchpoints (T1-T7) → verdict drives ship / fix-forward / STOP**. No commit lands on a design surface without codex's verdict on file.

.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:219:# Reference: `https://resources.anthropic.com/2026-agentic-coding-trends-report` (TIER-1 Anthropic; full PDF at `https://resources.anthropic.com/hubfs/2026%20Agentic%20Coding%20Trends%20Report.pdf`) [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via Wave-6 Agent M perplexity_research [17][31][48]] — Anthropic 2026 Agentic Coding Trends Report identifies risk-stratified gating as the SOTA pattern for AI-agent code change verification: low-risk = full-agent-delegate + post-commit review; medium-risk = pre-commit human review (agent implements, human approves); high-risk = design review BEFORE agent implements.
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:228:| **Low-risk** | Reversible operations on isolated surfaces (tests / docs / non-cardinal-rule edits / bounded scripts ≤200 LOC where convergence is well-understood); single-axis-change with clear primary-source backing | Full-agent-delegate + **post-commit review only** (T3 PostToolUse `Bash(git commit *)`) | T1 SKIP per §"Mechanical-mirror exception" at `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` IF predicates met; else light T1 + T3 verify |
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:229:| **Medium-risk** | Multi-file ships / new mechanics / behavioral changes / existing-surface edits with potential ripple; rule-layer or skill-layer additions ≤200 LOC | Pre-commit cross-model review (**T2 commit-time hook** at `Bash(git commit *)` PreToolUse — agent implements, codex GPT-5.5 reviews uncommitted tree before commit lands) | T1 pre-edit + T2 working-tree + T3 post-commit (full lifecycle) |
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:230:| **High-risk** | Cardinal-rule surface edits (CLAUDE.md / `.claude/settings.json` / `.mcp.json` / cardinal-rules-defining `.claude/rules/*.md`) / security-class operations / destructive Bash / >200 LOC architectural changes / new T-touchpoint or wedge-class introduction | **Design review BEFORE agent implements** (T0 candidate-list challenge + T1 deep-review-exec xhigh + T5 plan-stage; multi-cohort fan-out + Probe-DAG harness-fit verification BEFORE T1 fires) | T0 + T5 (pre-design) + T1 (pre-edit) + T2 (pre-commit) + T3 (post-commit) + T4 (post-push) + T6 (session-end) — full 7-touchpoint coverage |
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:234:1. **Classify task risk-class FIRST** (BEFORE writing T1 consult or scoping the ship):
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:240:3. **Document risk-class in commit body** — include `risk-class: low | medium | high` line + 1-line classification rationale (e.g., `risk-class: medium — multi-file rule edit + new mechanics introduced; T1+T2+T3 lifecycle invoked`)
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:241:4. **Risk-class is REVISABLE pre-commit** — if T1 surfaces unanticipated cardinal-surface impact (e.g., the rule edit changes existing T-touchpoint semantics rather than adding a new section), upgrade to high-risk and fire T0+T5 retroactively before commit
.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:245:- **"All changes go through medium-risk gate"** — refuted by Anthropic taxonomy (Wave-6 Agent M source). Low-risk operations (test files, doc-only updates, additive bounded sections with no behavior change) are over-gated by medium-risk T1+T2 coverage; the medium-risk full lifecycle costs ~5-10 min per ship unnecessarily. Conversely, high-risk operations (CLAUDE.md cardinal-rule trim, settings.json env block changes, .mcp.json server addition) are under-gated by medium-risk-only T1+T2 — they need T0 candidate-list challenge + T5 plan-stage review BEFORE T1 fires.

.claude/rules\cross-model-consensus.md:314:- **Trust first T1 verdict hit instead of file END** — codex deep-review-exec outputs span 1k-10k lines and routinely echo the `VERDICT: APPROVE | NEEDS-REVISION | REJECT` template near the start before the actual investigation, then emit the real verdict at EOF. Discipline: `wc -l <file>` first; then `grep -nE "VERDICT:|^F-[0-9]|conf=|confidence|APPROVE|NEEDS-REVISION|REJECT|severity (P[0-3])" <file> | tail -20` (force last hits, not first); then `tail -200 <file>` and confirm the final verdict block is at EOF. Genuine-truncation branch: only call `INCONCLUSIVE-INCOMPLETE` when the last grep hits are prompt-echo / early-investigation AND `tail -200` ends mid-investigation with no final verdict — high line count alone does NOT imply completion. Empirically refuted on iter-17/18/20 completed T1 verdict files (n=3 verdicts at L4457/L10073/L1610 + EOF) plus iter-19 separate skipped-T1 propagation case — total n=4 affected commit-body claims, n=3 verified completed outputs. Failure class: `synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Reporting categories OVER. Operational home: this rule's verdict-reading + fail-closed protocol. Provenance: `feedback_codex_t1_verdict_reading_discipline.md` + iter-21 fix-forward.

.claude/rules\parallel-session-worktree-isolation.md:115:   - **(c) Lockfile + O_CREAT|O_APPEND atomic-write for SHARED JSONL append**: open the JSONL log with `os.open(path, os.O_CREAT | os.O_APPEND | os.O_WRONLY, mode)` where `mode` defaults to `0o600` (owner-only — per Round-5b T3 round-3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.78 NEW-CLASS trust-boundary finding on commit `c2b5dee`: `0o644` was world-readable and leaked audit-trail content (agent prompts, PIDs, verdict cite paths, `agent_id` UUIDs, T1/T2/T3 verdict bodies) on multi-user hosts. Callers MAY opt-in to `0o640` group-readable for shared-CI cases via explicit `mode=` argument + documented umask interaction (e.g., `umask 0o027` for CI runner) — `0o644` is REJECTED as default for any audit-trail JSONL). **Migration semantic for legacy files** *(Round-7 amendment per T3 round-4 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.91 NEW-CLASS finding on commit `df37fb1`; closes non-migrating gap caught by codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE — POSIX `os.open` applies `mode` ONLY when `O_CREAT` actually CREATES the file)*: pre-existing files at `0o644` (created under Round-3-through-5 mandates pre-Round-6) RETAIN their world-readable perms after open, leaving the audit-trail leak intact unless coerced. After every `os.open`, the caller MUST: (i) read `current_mode = stat.S_IMODE(os.fstat(fd).st_mode)`; (ii) if `current_mode != mode` AND the file is owned by caller-uid, call `os.fchmod(fd, mode)` to coerce existing file to the mode invariant; (iii) if `os.fchmod` raises (`PermissionError` due to read-only mount, alien owner, ACL conflict on Windows NTFS, etc.), FAIL CLOSED — propagate the exception rather than proceeding with non-conforming perms (the audit-trail invariant is enforced everywhere or fails loudly). Document in commit body for any consumer applying this rule: one-time migration shell command `find .claude/state -name '*.jsonl' -perm /044 -exec chmod 0600 {} +` (POSIX) OR `Get-ChildItem -Path .claude/state -Filter *.jsonl -Recurse | Where-Object { ($_ | Get-Acl).AccessToString -match 'Everyone Allow Read' } | ForEach-Object { icacls $_.FullName /inheritance:r /grant ${env:USERNAME}:F }` (Windows) for legacy `mcp_health.jsonl` / `subagent_transcripts.jsonl` / `codex_postcommit_reviews.jsonl` / `subagent_start.jsonl` / `worktree_lifecycle_audit.jsonl` files created at `0o644` per pre-Round-6 mandates. The `O_CREAT` flag ensures a missing log auto-creates rather than failing the first writer; **if `os.open` raises (read-only mount, permission denied, disk full), the exception MUST propagate — do not catch-and-ignore** (per Round-5 F-1 silent-failure finding). Take an **exclusive advisory lock** (`fcntl.flock(fd, LOCK_EX)` on POSIX / `msvcrt.locking` on Windows); **if neither primitive is available, or if the platform does not enforce advisory locks (e.g., NFS without NLM support), FAIL CLOSED — raise rather than proceeding without a lock** (per Round-5 F-2 NFS-silent-succeed finding). **Windows separate-lockfile mechanism** *(Round-7c per T3 round-7b NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.78 NEW-CLASS finding on commit `48c49fb` caught by codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE)*: `msvcrt.locking` locks `nbytes` from CURRENT file-pointer position, NOT the whole file like POSIX `fcntl.flock`. Under O_APPEND the JSONL fd's pointer moves on each write, breaking lock-release symmetry (LK_UNLCK at the new pointer would find no lock at the byte LK_LOCK was issued for). Round-7b workaround (shipped at `scripts/_atomic_jsonl_append.py:_acquire_lock(fd, path)` commit `48c49fb`): open separate `<jsonl-path>.lock` file with 1-byte placeholder + lock byte 0 of the LOCKFILE (no writes to lockfile means file pointer stays at byte 0). Module returns `lock_fd` for Windows callers to release; `None` for POSIX. **Lock-protocol rollout constraint** *(Round-7c per same T3 finding)*: when the lock protocol bumps (direct-jsonl-fd → separate-lockfile-v1, or separate-lockfile-v1 → separate-lockfile-v2-versioned), enforce single-version deployment for the entire concurrent-writer cohort. Old + new code locking DIFFERENT lock domains do NOT mutually exclude — concurrent writers can race even though each ALONE acquires its lock correctly. Mitigations (pick ONE before any concurrent rollout): **(i) transitional dual-lock**: acquire both old AND new protocol locks in series during the transition window; **(ii) runtime version-marker**: write protocol-version byte to lockfile + fail-closed on read-version-mismatch (FORWARD-REF for v2 lock protocol bump); **(iii) single-version cohort barrier**: coordinate cohort upgrade via deployment system before any new-version runs. Currently sss has ZERO consumers of `_atomic_jsonl_append.py` (FORWARD-REF wire-up Ships 2-5 pending per `tmp/wave22-orchestrator-salvage-jsonl-mode-bits-audit-2026-05-05.md`), so the version-skew window is THEORETICAL until first hook migrates to use the module. Future protocol bumps MUST honor one of (i)/(ii)/(iii). HOLD the lock across BOTH (a) JSON-encoding the record AND (b) the single low-level `os.write(fd, encoded_bytes)` — never use buffered text writes (`f.write(json.dumps(rec) + "\n")` via Python file objects can split into multiple writes). **If JSON encoding raises (`UnicodeEncodeError`, non-serializable type), release the lock and propagate the exception — do not silently drop the record** (per Round-5 F-3 silent-record-drop finding). For records where **`len(encoded_bytes)` — the byte count AFTER JSON encoding, NOT the key count of the dict —** exceeds the platform atomic-write bound (≤PIPE_BUF 4KB POSIX; ≤512 bytes for Windows or treat the lock as the sole correctness boundary), reject the record OR shard via option (b) filename-namespace; do NOT silently truncate (per Round-5 F-5 measurement-target finding). After the write, `os.fsync(fd)` if durability is part of the contract (audit-trail JSONL needs fsync; ephemeral telemetry may skip); **if `os.fsync` raises (`EIO`, `ENOSPC`), propagate the exception — do not silently ignore a durability failure when durability is contracted** (per Round-5 F-4 fsync-failure finding). Use for SHARED JSONL log targets (`mcp_health.jsonl`, `subagent_transcripts.jsonl`, `codex_postcommit_reviews.jsonl` class) where consumers expect interleaved appends from multiple writers and unified-view semantics are required.

.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:16:# SOTA-pin discipline
.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:18:# Source: parent-port with split claude-sota infrastructure status from `Z:/claude/rules/sota-pin-discipline.md` @ parent-ccc `feeefdfacb05d83b2ec77fbb4c2da2fb9ca41f5b` [VERIFIED 2026-05-02]. The SOTA-pin discipline and four-stage Wire/Surface/Close/Re-fire loop are parent-derived; claude-sota's `PINS.json` manifest is LOCAL at `Z:/claude-sota-installed/PINS.json`, while repo-local `sota_freshness_sweep` and `sota_pin_audit` remain FORWARD-REF/P3 and parent external-cadence runs are TIER-3 provenance only.
.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:21:A SOTA-pin (model SHA, tool version, plugin version, MCP inventory baseline, cited-repo HEAD SHA) is a claim about external state. Without mechanized re-verification, the claim drifts silently — `[VERIFIED 2026-04-25]` becomes `[VERIFIED-but-actually-stale]` within days. The chassis is `PINS.json` (manifest, LOCAL at `Z:/claude-sota-installed/PINS.json`) + `sota_freshness_sweep` script (audit, FORWARD-REF for repo-local; TIER-3 external-cadence provenance currently exists only via parent Z:/claude/scripts/sota_freshness_sweep.py) + `sota_pin_audit` hook (SessionStart consumer, FORWARD-REF for P3).
.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:23:This rule is the consumer-side discipline parallel to `.claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md`, scoped specifically to SOTA-reference freshness rather than count-claim drift.
.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:34:Whenever a claimed SOTA reference (categories below) doesn't match the actual environment or has gone unverified longer than the per-category freshness window. The first 4 categories are Tier-1 (currently in scope when the audit lands). The 5th category (cited-repo HEADs) is **Tier-3/queued** — schema field defined, but its probe is intentionally OUT of the wired audit per ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE.
.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:44:Tier-2 (star counts + cpd, axis-3 expiry timers) and Tier-3 (eval scores via WebFetch, citation-anchor resolution) are explicitly OUT of the wired audit — queued for follow-up cycles.
.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:56:5. **Atomic batch is OPTIONAL here** — unlike `.claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md` count-claim drift (which needs atomic-batch with the ship that introduced the count change), SOTA-pin bumps are typically standalone events. Bundle only if multiple categories drifted simultaneously from the same root cause (e.g. weekly external update wave).
.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:60:SOTA references decay faster than code references. A function rename triggers a search-and-replace; a model-SHA rebake or a `pyrefly 0.61.1 → 0.62.0` upstream bump produces zero local symptoms — until someone runs the audit. Unwired audits miss this entirely; wired-but-unactioned audits accumulate noise and stop being trusted. The 4-stage discipline (Wire → Surface → Close → Re-fire) is what converts "we have an audit" into "the SOTA stack is current."
.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:68:- **Network-probe scope creep** — Tier-2 (star counts) and Tier-3 (citation-anchor content equality) intentionally use NO network. Don't add `gh api` calls into the SessionStart hot path; queue for separate audit script.
.claude/rules\sota-pin-discipline.md:73:- `.claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md` — local sibling pattern for wired-audit closure discipline; this rule scopes the same 4-stage shape to SOTA-reference freshness specifically. Audit-action-loop documents the TIER-3 external-cadence distinction this rule inherits.

.claude/rules\sota-research-architecture.md:7:# SOTA Research Architecture (SRA) — multi-dimensional convergence gate
.claude/rules\sota-research-architecture.md:9:# Reference: TIER-1-DIRECT user directive 2026-05-08 verbatim "improve your research architecture, you need to make sure they have the ability to gather muti dimensional informaiton, github and beyond, to find the convergence, it is the very foundation of our entire architecutre" — codifies the methodology improvement mandate triggered by Wave 102 audit's over-applied REJECT verdicts (Ship 2T trufflehog AGPL-3.0 over-applied to CLI-binary-use; KuzuDB recommended at 7-month-stale despite "SOTA" framing).
.claude/rules\sota-research-architecture.md:11:# Reference: TIER-2 sister `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/convergence-gate.md` — Axis-1+2+3 convergence-gate (org-distinct + named-T2 + ≥3-month stable). SRA EXTENDS that 3-axis gate to 10-dimension convergence with use-class precision.
.claude/rules\sota-research-architecture.md:13:# Reference: TIER-2 sister `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md` Probe 6 license-blocker — SRA D1 dimension is the use-class-precise refinement of Probe 6 (Probe 6 is blanket REJECT on AGPL/GPL/SSPL; SRA D1 distinguishes CLI-binary-use vs library-link vs network-served vs SaaS-distributed).
.claude/rules\sota-research-architecture.md:17:A repo qualifies as **SOTA** for eee runtime adoption if and only if it satisfies **multi-dimensional convergence** across 10 independent dimensions, with **use-class precision** per dimension. Flat criteria ("AGPL = REJECT" / "stars >500") produce false-positive rejects + miss real risk. Convergence gate = ≥7 of 10 dimensions PASS for use-class, with no critical dimension (D1 license-use-class blocking, D6 use-class incompatible) FAILED.
.claude/rules\sota-research-architecture.md:19:**Replacement viability**: any proposed replacement MUST satisfy D1-D10 AND be ≤ incumbent's last-push age (D2 freshness gate). A staler replacement is NOT a valid SOTA recommendation.

.claude/rules\team-orchestration.md:284:**Snapshot-labeling + recovery-pointer discipline [SSS-local]**: when a tool output is truncated under budget, the emitter MUST label the snapshot (`[truncated at N tokens]`) AND name the recovery tool to fetch the full output (`call X with id Y for complete output`). Silent truncation forces the model to infer absence vs limit. Applies to `.claude/hooks/scripts/` printing to stderr under sys.exit(2) rewake and to Agent tool results exceeding `MAX_AGENT_OUTPUT`. (Prior cite to `awesome-agentic-patterns/patterns/context-window-auto-compaction.md:1` removed per codex T1 G-024 F-003 — that pattern covers context overflow auto-compaction, not truncated-output recovery pointers; the snapshot-labeling rule is sss-internal discipline.)
.claude/rules\team-orchestration.md:286:**Context-memoization invalidation gotcha [SSS-local]**: progressive-disclosure rule files + component CLAUDE.md are memoized per session. Adding a new context source (`.claude/rules/foo.md`, `.claude/agents/bar.md`, skill) WITHOUT updating its referring index leaves the model with a stale view for the rest of the session. # SOTA Research Architecture (SRA) — multi-dimensional convergence gate

# Reference: TIER-1-DIRECT user directive 2026-05-08 verbatim "improve your research architecture, you need to make sure they have the ability to gather muti dimensional informaiton, github and beyond, to find the convergence, it is the very foundation of our entire architecutre" — codifies the methodology improvement mandate triggered by Wave 102 audit's over-applied REJECT verdicts (Ship 2T trufflehog AGPL-3.0 over-applied to CLI-binary-use; KuzuDB recommended at 7-month-stale despite "SOTA" framing).

# Reference: TIER-2 sister `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/convergence-gate.md` — Axis-1+2+3 convergence-gate (org-distinct + named-T2 + ≥3-month stable). SRA EXTENDS that 3-axis gate to 10-dimension convergence with use-class precision.

# Reference: TIER-2 sister `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md` Probe 6 license-blocker — SRA D1 dimension is the use-class-precise refinement of Probe 6 (Probe 6 is blanket REJECT on AGPL/GPL/SSPL; SRA D1 distinguishes CLI-binary-use vs library-link vs network-served vs SaaS-distributed).

## The rule

A repo qualifies as **SOTA** for eee runtime adoption if and only if it satisfies **multi-dimensional convergence** across 10 independent dimensions, with **use-class precision** per dimension. Flat criteria ("AGPL = REJECT" / "stars >500") produce false-positive rejects + miss real risk. Convergence gate = ≥7 of 10 dimensions PASS for use-class, with no critical dimension (D1 license-use-class blocking, D6 use-class incompatible) FAILED.

**Replacement viability**: any proposed replacement MUST satisfy D1-D10 AND be ≤ incumbent's last-push age (D2 freshness gate). A staler replacement is NOT a valid SOTA recommendation.

## When this rule applies

ANY of these triggers fires SRA:

1. Architecture audit identifying REJECT/DOWNGRADE candidate
2. New repo/dep adoption decision
3. Replacement of existing dep with alternative
4. Plugin install candidate evaluation
5. License-context question (any non-MIT / non-Apache / non-BSD license)
6. SOTA-freshness verification of any cite anchor
7. Named-author endorsement claim verification

## The 10 dimensions

### D1 — License-use-class precision

Distinguish use-class BEFORE applying license clauses. License clause activation per sub-class:

| License | CLI-binary-use | Library-link | Network-served | SaaS-distributed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MIT / Apache-2.0 / BSD | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| LGPL-2.1/3.0 | ✅ | dynamic-link OK; static-link infects | ✅ | ✅ |
| GPL-2.0/3.0 | ✅ (no source-distribute mandate) | infects derivative | requires source-disclosure | full GPL-class restrictions |
| AGPL-3.0 | ✅ (local-CLI use) | infects derivative + network-trigger | requires source-disclosure | full AGPL-class restrictions |
| SSPL-1.0 | ✅ (local DB) | ✅ | ✅ | restricts SaaS-as-product |
| Elastic License 2.0 (ELv2) | ✅ (local plugin) | ✅ | ✅ | restricts managed-service-resale |
| Proprietary (commercial) | per-license-terms | per-license-terms | per-license-terms | per-license-terms |
| **NO LICENSE FILE** | undefined; default copyright; technically no permission to use | undefined | undefined | undefined |

**Probe 6 application** is use-class-aware:
- AGPL CLI binary used to scan local files = ✅ ACCEPTABLE for eee
- AGPL library linked into eee runtime code = ❌ REJECT (license infects derivative)
- AGPL network-served as part of eee = ❌ REJECT (requires source-disclosure)
- ELv2 plugin loaded into eee runtime = ✅ ACCEPTABLE (eee is not SaaS-resale)
- SSPL DB used as local backend = ✅ ACCEPTABLE (eee is not DB-as-a-service product)

**eee use-class baseline**: local autonomous /loop runtime; NOT distributed-as-product; NOT network-hosted-for-third-parties; NOT SaaS-resale. This use-class makes most non-permissive licenses ACCEPTABLE for inclusion (with disclosure).

### D2 — SOTA-freshness gate (replacement-must-be-more-current)

For any proposed REPLACEMENT:
- Compute `incumbent_last_push_age` = (now - incumbent's pushed_at)
- Compute `replacement_last_push_age` = (now - replacement's pushed_at)
- **MUST**: replacement_last_push_age ≤ incumbent_last_push_age
- IF replacement is staler than incumbent → REJECT REPLACEMENT, keep incumbent

For incumbents:
- last-push < 30 days = ACTIVE
- 30-90 days = MAINTAINED
- 90-180 days = STABLE-BURN-IN (acceptable for mature deps)
- 180-365 days = STALE (DOWNGRADE flag)
- > 365 days = DORMANT (REJECT unless explicit ABANDONED-BUT-WORKING justification)

### D3 — Star-velocity vs content-depth (fresh-paint detection)

Calculate signals:
- `velocity = stars / age-days`
- `density = repo-size-KB / max(stars, 1)`

Anti-pattern signals:
- `velocity > 100 stars/day` AND `density < 0.5 KB/star` → FRESH-PAINT SUSPECT
- Squashed git history + age <30 days + 1K+ stars → FRESH-PAINT (per `convergence-gate.md` §Anti-pattern)
- High star count BUT no named-practitioner endorsement → DOWNGRADE

Verify: Read README. Confirm content matches star count. Inspect git history for organic vs squashed-import pattern.

### D4 — Maintainer provenance tier

| Tier | Definition | Convergence weight |
|---|---|---|
| **TIER-1-OFFICIAL** | Anthropic / OpenAI / Microsoft / Google / Apache / Linux Foundation / etc | STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS |
| **TIER-2-NAMED-PRACTITIONER** | Karpathy / Pocock / Osmani / Cherny / named-author with public track record | strong |
| **TIER-3-NAMED-ORG** | small org with named maintainers + multi-contributor | acceptable |
| **TIER-4-NAMED-INDIVIDUAL** | single named individual + active commit history | acceptable-with-disclosure |
| **TIER-5-UNKNOWN** | anonymous OR single-org with no named practitioners | DOWNGRADE; verify before adoption |

**NO LICENSE FILE on TIER-1 or TIER-2 repo**: not a REJECT — verify the named-author intent (e.g., GitHub README declaration "MIT" without LICENSE file is COMMON for prototypes; cite-import is acceptable with disclosure).

### D5 — Active maintenance signals (beyond last-push)

- Issue-close-rate (closed / opened over rolling 90-day window) > 30% = active
- PR-merge-rate (merged / opened) > 40% = active
- Contributor diversity (number of distinct contributors past 90 days) > 1 = healthy
- Recent release cadence (releases per 90 days) > 0 = shipping

If 0 of 4 signals = ABANDONED; if 1-2 = STALE-MAYBE-MAINTAINED (probe further); if 3-4 = ACTIVE.

### D6 — Use-class compatibility (mode-harness-shape Probe 5)

Does the repo's INTENDED use-mode match eee runtime?

| Repo mode | eee compatibility |
|---|---|
| Autonomous /loop runtime | ✅ |
| Human-in-loop interactive | ✅ (subagent or operator-direct) |
| HARD-GATE pause-resume only | ❌ (incompatible with autonomous /loop; e.g., superpowers brainstorming) |
| Meta-skill TDD-for-skills harness | ❌ if claude-sota lacks TDD-for-skills test framework (e.g., superpowers writing-skills) |
| TIER-1 OFFICIAL CC plugin format | ✅ (Anthropic-curated for CC runtime) |
| Standalone CLI tool | ✅ (binary use-class) |
| Library/SDK requiring code-link | ✅ if license-link compatible (D1) |

### D7 — Anthropic CC official policy alignment

Probe Anthropic's actual policy:
- `code.claude.com/docs/en/*` — explicit license policy statements
- `anthropics/claude-plugins-official` marketplace — does Anthropic ship plugins under similar license? (If Anthropic ships X plugin under SSPL, SSPL is OK by Anthropic standards.)
- Anthropic CHANGELOG — license-acceptability statements
- `cookbook.anthropic.com` — examples ship under what licenses

If Anthropic OFFICIALLY ships under license X for similar use-case → license X is acceptable. Don't apply stricter standard than Anthropic itself.

### D8 — Industry adoption signal

- Multi-org adoption: ≥3 distinct orgs use this repo in production
- Named-practitioner endorsements with dated artifact (tweet / blog / talk) ≥ 2
- Conference talks / academic papers cite it
- Posted to mailing lists / official Anthropic Discord / GitHub Discussions / forums

A repo with high stars but ZERO industry adoption signals = popularity without depth.

### D9 — Failure-mode awareness

Per `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/named-failure-modes.md` catalog:
- Known FM-class triggered by this repo
- Recovery path documented OR pattern-match against existing FM
- CVE / security advisories
- Issue tracker patterns

A repo with documented FM-class still ACCEPTABLE if recovery path is clear; UNDOCUMENTED-FAIL-CLASS = DOWNGRADE.

### D10 — Replacement viability (when proposing alternatives)

If proposing X → Y replacement:
- Y must satisfy D1-D9 INDEPENDENTLY for the use-class
- Y must satisfy D2 freshness gate (Y_last_push_age ≤ X_last_push_age)
- Y must FUNCTIONALLY cover same surface as X (not just claim coverage)
- Y must have D7 Anthropic-aligned position OR named-T1 cite

**A replacement that is staler than the incumbent is NOT a valid recommendation.** Period.

## Convergence verdict (decision lattice)

Score each repo across D1-D10:
- D1 PASS for use-class = +1 (CRITICAL — D1 FAIL = blocking)
- D2 freshness PASS = +1
- D3 fresh-paint clear = +1
- D4 maintainer-provenance ≥ TIER-3 = +1
- D5 active-maintenance signals ≥ 2/4 = +1
- D6 use-class compatible = +1 (CRITICAL — D6 FAIL = blocking)
- D7 Anthropic-aligned OR not-required = +1
- D8 industry adoption ≥ 1 = +1
- D9 FM-class clear OR documented-recovery = +1
- D10 replacement gate (if applicable) PASS = +1

**Verdict thresholds**:

| Score | Verdict | Action |
|---|---|---|
| 9-10 + critical D1+D6 PASS | **INSTALL** | adopt; full SOTA confirmed |
| 7-8 + critical D1+D6 PASS | **DOWNGRADE-WITH-DISCLOSURE** | adopt with documentation of caveats |
| 5-6 OR critical D1/D6 ambiguous | **DEFER** | needs more probes (HONEST-NON-FINDING) |
| <5 OR critical D1+D6 FAIL | **REJECT** | does not satisfy SRA |

## Cross-model T1 verification mandate

Per CR-3 + Ship 2P fully-unleashed mandate: **ANY** SRA verdict involving REJECT or REPLACEMENT MUST be cross-model-verified via real GPT-5.5 codex T1 BEFORE any commit. Sonnet stand-in agent does RESEARCH (D1-D10 probe outputs); codex GPT-5.5 does VERIFICATION (does the SRA verdict actually hold?).

Failure of this mandate produces over-applied REJECTs (Wave 102 lesson — trufflehog AGPL-3.0 over-applied to CLI-binary-use).

## Anti-patterns (from Wave 102 audit lessons)

1. **Flat license REJECT without use-class context** — Wave 102 over-applied AGPL-3.0/SSPLv1/ELv2 REJECTs to local-runtime use. SRA D1 is use-class-precise.
2. **Recommend stale replacement** — Wave 102 recommended KuzuDB v0.11.3 (Oct 2025) as FalkorDB replacement when FalkorDB is more current. SRA D2+D10 prevents this.
3. **NO LICENSE FILE = REJECT without verifying named-author intent** — Wave 102 flagged forrestchang/andrej-karpathy-skills as authority-risk. SRA D4 distinguishes named-T2 with README-stated license (acceptable) vs unknown anonymous (REJECT).
4. **Star-low = DOWNGRADE without industry-adoption probe** — Wave 102 flagged 201★ cnighswonger as caveat. SRA D3 + D5 + D8 disambiguate "popular but stale" from "low-star but active and adopted".
5. **Skip cross-model T1 on audit methodology** — Wave 102 audit was Sonnet stand-in only; never cross-verified. Ship 2X mandates T1 e2e on SRA verdicts.
6. **No replacement-freshness gate** — Wave 102 recommended replacements without verifying replacement is more current. D2 + D10 codify this.
7. **Apply stricter license standard than Anthropic itself** — Wave 102 didn't probe D7. If Anthropic ships under license X, license X is acceptable.

## Update triggers

# Agent harness-fit verification — axis-4 of convergence-gate

# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/projects/Z--claude-sota-installed/memory/feedback_sota_researcher_harness_fit_verification_2026_05_02.md (n=5 promotion-source memory; this rule is the codification per `.claude/rules/codification-threshold.md` cycle-322 jurisdiction n>=3 self-observed promotion bar)
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/convergence-gate.md (Axis 1+2+3 — this rule adds Axis 4 harness-fit as the scoring layer agents currently miss)
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator (UPSTREAM-CLAIM / OPERATIONAL-CLAIM / CATEGORY-CLAIM — agents must classify candidate type before harness-fit probe)
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/codification-threshold.md cycle-322 jurisdiction (n>=3 self-observed promotes feedback→rule; n=5 satisfied)
# Reference: TIER-2 .claude/rules/kiss-dry-yagni.md Must-Never #4 (no duplicate functionality — plugin-namespace duplicates land here as primary class)
# TIER-3 evidence-trail provenance only: `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota-installed/memory/feedback_sota_researcher_harness_fit_verification_2026_05_02.md` carries the 5-instance evidence ladder + per-class analysis; this rule is the operational mechanism that file's evidence promotes.

## The rule

Sota-researcher / architect / general agent dispatches that surface SOTA candidates MUST include a **harness-fit verification step** asking "is this applicable to OUR specific architecture?" — beyond the convergence-gate Axis 1+2+3 (T1 sources / T2 practitioners / Axis 3 stability) that scores SOTA-status. Without harness-fit, candidates pass convergence-gate AND still fail at ship-target verification because of architectural mismatch invisible to upstream-source-only audit.

This is **convergence-gate Axis 4** (harness-fit) — separate from Axis 1+2+3 (which classify SOTA-status of the candidate ITSELF) and applied AFTER convergence-gate PASS, BEFORE ship-target verification.

## When this rule applies

Whenever an agent (sota-researcher / architect / general-purpose / Explore / code-reviewer / debugger) returns a ship-candidate ranking from a Wave-N dispatch:

1. The agent's convergence-gate verdict is necessary BUT NOT SUFFICIENT for ship-readiness
2. The candidate may be valid SOTA AND inapplicable to claude-sota
3. Apply the 4-axis harness-fit probe BEFORE writing T1 consult prompt OR queuing the ship

## The 7 sub-classes (n=10 evidence ladder per source memory + FM-09 promotion iter-67 + Probe 7 promotion 2026-05-03)

| Class | Probe | Failure shape | n |
|---|---|---|---|
| **count-OVER** | `git log` actual count vs `mcp__github__list_commits` agent-claimed count | iter-71→73: agent claimed 14 commits stale, actual 1 cosmetic commit | 1 |
| **SDK-vs-CLI surface** | `Glob` for the agent-claimed invocation surface (config flag, env var, hook script) | Ship 1: `exclude_dynamic_sections=True` exists in SDK but only via PRINT-MODE flag; sss runs interactive — NO invocation surface | 1 |
| **architectural-API** | Cross-check candidate's API against actual call shape | Ship 2: `cache_control` is Anthropic-API; codex CLI is OpenAI-flavored — architectural mismatch | 1 |
| **plugin-namespace** | Check loaded `<plugin>:*` skill namespace BEFORE recommending vendoring of upstream artifact | iter-3 Agent C: `everything-claude-code:rules-distill` AND `everything-claude-code:agent-introspection-debugging` already plugin-exposed — vendoring would DUPLICATE per kiss-dry-yagni Must-Never #4 | 2 |
| **mode-harness-shape** | Cross-check candidate's operational mode (HARD-GATE / interactive-vs-autonomous / meta-skill harness assumption / size-sprawl) against claude-sota's actual harness shape | iter-84 brainstorming: HARD-GATE blocks ALL implementation until interactive user approval — incompatible with autonomous /loop mode; iter-85 writing-skills: 7+ files / 100KB+ size-sprawl AND meta-skill assumes Superpowers-specific TDD-for-skills harness which claude-sota does not have | 2 |
| **direct-file/registry blockers** (Probe 6, FM-09 promotion iter-67) | Direct file reads (LICENSE / README-badge / archive-status banner) AND remote registry direct-existence probe (npm/PyPI/cargo) for candidates whose viability depends on upstream artifact existence + license-compatibility | Memory/RAG audit: openviking REJECT-FOR-FIT 0.92 — `Z:/repos/deps/openviking/LICENSE:1-2` "GNU AFFERO GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE Version 3" + README:19 AGPLv3 badge ([VERIFIED 2026-05-02 fresh probe]); claude-sota is permissive-license-only — STRUCTURAL adoption blocker. Ship D ast-grep MCP: phantom `@anthropic/mcp-ast-grep` package — `https://registry.npmjs.org/@anthropic%2Fmcp-ast-grep` returns `{"error":"Not found"}` ([VERIFIED 2026-05-02 fresh probe]); CC3 `.claude/mcp_config.json:62` had `"disabled": true` — now we know why | 2 |
| **demand-gate split** (Probe 7, 2026-05-03 dbhub; .a/.b split 2026-05-05 Wave 27) | Audit harness-side operational driver: does sss have any current or queued workflow / invocation / consumer that would route through this primitive? Probe 7.a (DEMAND-ABSENCE → REJECT) and Probe 7.b (DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW → STUDY-PILOT eligible after 5-clause check). | bytebase/dbhub REJECT-FOR-FIT 0.85 — structurally sound MCP (MIT + STABLE-BURN-IN + lean 2-tool surface + Bytebase named-org) BUT sss has NO application-DB query workflow. Existing DB-adjacent primitives cover live adjacent uses: `mcp-memory` uses sqlite-vec storage, `graphiti` uses FalkorDB temporal KG, Langfuse data flows through Langfuse MCP. Retro corroboration: `apollo-graphql` is line-cited as demand-absence-shaped per parent `Z:/claude/.claude/projects/Z--claude/memory/reference_mcp_disabled_rationale.md:20` (no GraphQL API/schema/endpoint in harness). NOT counted: `claude-context` (credential-gated MILVUS_TOKEN), `grafana` (A/B-pending after adoption downgrade), `opik` (credential-gated OPIK_API_KEY) — these are CREDENTIAL-GATED-PENDING / A/B-PENDING distinct classes, not demand-absence. Disabled MCP inventory at `manifests/services.yaml:80` + `.claude/settings.json:217` shows the slot-occupation risk pattern. **Wave 27 caveat (2026-05-05)**: dbhub may be DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW.b for audit-log SQL analytics, but only with an explicit JSONL → SQLite/Postgres ETL path. DBHub does not currently provide direct JSONL querying per `https://dbhub.ai/` + `bytebase/dbhub@72adfdcf` README + `server.json` — supports Postgres/MySQL/SQL Server/MariaDB/SQLite via SQL tools only. | 1 fresh + 1 retro |

Total: n=10 instances, n=7 distinct classes — promotion-eligible per `codification-threshold.md` cycle-322 jurisdiction (n>=3 bar; FM-09 ladder satisfies independently at n=5 per §Codex-rescue blind-spot specialization below).

## How to apply (4-axis probe)

Before declaring an agent's ship-candidate "ready":

### Probe 1 — count-OVER

If the candidate claims a count delta (commit count, file count, line count, star count): verify against authoritative local source (`git log`, `wc -l`, `Glob | wc -l`, `mcp__github__search_repositories` star refresh) BEFORE accepting. Agent counts via remote APIs can drift, paginate-truncate, or include stale cache.

### Probe 2 — SDK-vs-CLI surface

If the candidate proposes adopting an SDK feature / config flag / env var: verify the **invocation surface** exists in claude-sota:
- Does claude-sota use the SDK or CLI? Check `.claude/hooks/scripts/*.py` + `.claude/settings.json` env block
- Does the candidate's mechanism map to claude-sota's actual mode (interactive vs print-mode vs SDK-managed-agent)?
- If the candidate requires a mode claude-sota doesn't run in, REJECT-for-fit even if SOTA-valid upstream

### Probe 3 — architectural-API

If the candidate proposes a primitive (cache_control, prompt-cache, structured-outputs, batch-API): verify the **API ecosystem match**:
- Anthropic-API primitive vs OpenAI-API primitive vs vendor-neutral OTel
- claude-sota's gpt5.5/codex hooks talk to OpenAI-API endpoints; Anthropic-only primitives don't apply there
- Cross-check before assuming "this Anthropic pattern works on codex"

### Probe 5 — mode-harness-shape

If the candidate proposes adopting a skill/workflow/pattern with operational mode dependencies (HARD-GATE on user interaction, interactive-vs-autonomous mismatch, meta-skill assumptions about sister-harness presence, file-count or size-sprawl orthogonal to existing layout): verify the **operational mode + harness shape match**:
- Does the candidate skill HARD-GATE on interactive user approval? Verify against claude-sota's autonomous /loop mode (incompatible)
- Does the candidate require a sister-skill or sister-harness present in upstream's framework but absent locally? (e.g., Superpowers TDD-for-skills test framework)
- Does verbatim port introduce file count or size disproportionate to existing layer? (e.g., a single skill bringing 7+ files when sibling skills are 1-3 files)
- If ANY mode/harness assumption is unmet, REJECT-FOR-FIT even if SOTA-valid upstream + Axis 1+2+3 PASS

### Probe 4 — plugin-namespace

If the candidate proposes vendoring an artifact from upstream (ECC, codex-plugin-cc, anthropics-cookbook): check **plugin-loaded skill namespace** FIRST:
- `everything-claude-code:<artifact>` exposed via plugin? → DUPLICATE if vendored, REJECT per kiss-dry-yagni Must-Never #4
- `codex:<artifact>` exposed via codex plugin? → DUPLICATE
- `<plugin>:<artifact>` only available as legacy-shim? → still plugin-exposed
- Only proceed with vendoring if the artifact is NOT in any loaded plugin namespace

**Where to check**: the available-skills list injected into agent context via system-reminder (lists every `<plugin>:<skill>` entry). Agents can also `mcp__github__get_file_contents` on the plugin's `marketplace.json` to enumerate exposed commands.

### Probe 6 — supplementary direct-file/registry blockers (FM-09 promotion iter-67)

If the candidate proposes adopting an upstream artifact (skill, MCP server, plugin, library, vendored pattern) whose viability depends on:
- License compatibility (claude-sota is permissive-license-only — MIT / Apache-2.0 / BSD acceptable; AGPLv3 / GPLv3 / SSPL / proprietary REJECT)
- README badge / status banner (archive-status, deprecation notice, "moved to maintenance mode" warning)
- Published-package existence (npm / PyPI / cargo / go-modules registry)
- Build-deps availability (Cargo / GCC / Clang / Python venv requirement)

VERIFY via direct-file/registry probes:
- **LICENSE file read** at upstream root: `cat Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/LICENSE | head -5` — match against permissive-license whitelist
- **README badge inspection**: `rg -n "img.shields.io/badge/license|maintenance|archived|deprecated" Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/README.md`
- **Registry direct-existence probe**: `curl -sS https://registry.npmjs.org/<pkg-encoded>` (`{"error":"Not found"}` = phantom) OR `curl https://pypi.org/pypi/<pkg>/json` (404 = phantom)
- **Build-deps probe**: `which cargo gcc clang` OR check `pyproject.toml` / `package.json` for unsupported dep stack

If ANY direct-file/registry probe surfaces a blocker, REJECT-FOR-FIT even if SOTA-valid upstream + Axis 1+2+3 PASS + Probe 1-5 PASS. The blocker is STRUCTURAL — no operational mitigation exists.

**Empirical evidence n=2 (FM-09 ladder)**: Memory/RAG audit (openviking AGPLv3 LICENSE blocker — `reference_memory_rag_audit_HNF_agplv3_blocker_2026_05_02.md:52-64`); Ship D (ast-grep MCP phantom @anthropic/mcp-ast-grep npm package — `reference_ship_d_ast_grep_phantom_npm_HNF_2026_05_02.md`).

### Probe 7 — demand-gate split (NEW 2026-05-03; .a/.b sub-class added 2026-05-05 Wave 27 per Agent E §Issue 1 catch-22)

If the candidate proposes adopting a primitive (MCP / skill / library / tool) that is structurally sound (Probes 1-6 PASS): verify the **harness-side operational driver** BEFORE adoption. Probe 7 splits into two outcomes — `.a` REJECT (demand-absence) and `.b` STUDY-PILOT eligible (demand-creates-new-workflow). The split closes the Wave 27 catch-22 surfaced at `tmp/wave27-agentE-saturation-adversarial-2026-05-05.md:46-56,89-106` (Probe 7.a as currently worded auto-REJECTs any primitive that creates a NEW workflow because no sss invocation surface yet exists — circular per Jeffries YAGNI canonical statement at `kiss-dry-yagni.md:31-33`).

**Hard precondition (before applying .a or .b)**: earlier Axis-4 blockers WIN. If the candidate duplicates an existing shipped primitive (Probe 4 plugin-namespace), collides with plugin namespace, requires an unmet harness/mode assumption (Probe 5 mode-harness-shape), fails Probe 6 (license/registry/build-deps blocker), is SUPERSEDED-BY-X (existing primitive owns the workflow), or is a META-HARNESS competing-framework shape per `docs/verified-avoid.md` Cohort 1, classify under that blocker instead. Probe 7.b is ONLY available for structurally sound primitives that create a genuinely new, non-duplicative workflow.

#### Probe 7.a — DEMAND-ABSENCE (REJECT-FOR-FIT)

What sss workflow / invocation surface / consumer would route through this primitive TODAY? Verify the 3-clause check:
- Cite a specific path (existing or queued)
- Is the candidate's value proposition met by an EXISTING shipped primitive? (e.g., "graphiti MCP already provides FalkorDB temporal KG access" → dbhub adds no marginal value AS A REPLACEMENT)
- Is the candidate displacing or extending? Displacement requires migration plan; extension requires marginal-value evidence

If NO current/queued workflow AND .b 5-clause check (below) does not survive, REJECT-FOR-FIT.a (demand-absence) — REJECT-decisive.

#### Probe 7.b — DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW (STUDY-PILOT eligible)

A structurally sound primitive may create a genuinely new workflow that no existing sss primitive serves. STUDY-PILOT eligibility requires ALL 5 clauses to hold (codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.84 Q2 prescription at `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship27_3_probe7_demand_creates_OUT.txt`):

1. **Named operational use case**: cite the query/workflow and the consumer that would exercise it
2. **Cited local input/source path**: identify the concrete data, repo path, tool surface, or state file (e.g., `Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/state/mcp_overhead_audit.jsonl`)
3. **Wiring path**: name the adapter/ETL/config needed to make the primitive consume the source; if wiring is non-trivial, it is part of the pilot cost
4. **Incumbent comparison**: explain why existing shipped primitives or simpler local commands (`rg`, `jq`, GitNexus, Langfuse MCP, graphiti, mcp-memory, one-off Python scripts) do not already satisfy the use case OR are materially worse
5. **Reversible time-box**: define owner, max pilot cost, 30-day success criterion, and retirement path

**Pilot failure**: retire to `disabledMcpjsonServers` if applicable and document in `docs/verified-avoid.md` under the matching failure cohort; create a dedicated retired-pilot / demand-not-realized cohort only after n>=2 same-shape recurrences. NOTE: `verified-avoid.md` Cohort 2 is specifically Probe 6 direct-file/registry blockers per `docs/verified-avoid.md:47-49`; failed demand-pilots do NOT belong there by default. Use the matching failure cohort for retirement (e.g., a pilot that failed because it duplicated an existing primitive retires to Cohort 1 META-HARNESS competing-frameworks).

**Example (dbhub Wave 27 caveat — ETL-explicit)**: bytebase/dbhub could enable SQL analytics over a generated SQLite audit warehouse derived from `.claude/state/*.jsonl`; the pilot must include the JSONL → SQLite ingestion path plus at least one committed query/report consumer. Without that ETL path, dbhub is NOT sufficient evidence for Probe 7.b — DBHub's official surface (`https://dbhub.ai/` + `bytebase/dbhub@72adfdcf` README + `server.json`) supports configured Postgres/MySQL/SQL Server/MariaDB/SQLite databases via SQL tools, NOT direct JSONL querying. Wave 27 codex T1 verified-via codex secondary probe of DBHub introduction page + TOML config docs + GitHub MCP-fetched README/server.json [VERIFIED 2026-05-05 via `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship27_3_probe7_demand_creates_OUT.txt`].

**Harness-fit discriminator for REJECT vs STUDY-PILOT outcomes** (renamed from "4-class harness-fit-FAIL discriminator" per Wave 27 codex T1 Q1 prescription — `.b` is non-fail; original Q3 prescription at `.claude/state/codex_consult_axis4_subclass_taxonomy_probe7_demand_absence_OUT.txt:3825-3829` 2026-05-03; .a/.b split per Wave 27 codex T1 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_ship27_3_probe7_demand_creates_OUT.txt` 2026-05-05): use BEFORE classifying a verdict to ensure the right Probe is invoked.

- `DEMAND-ABSENCE.a` (Probe 7.a): no current, queued, or counterfactual workflow survives the named-use-case check — REJECT-FOR-FIT
- `DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW.b` (Probe 7.b, NON-FAIL CLASS): structurally sound primitive creates a named, non-duplicative workflow; STUDY-PILOT eligible only after the 5-clause check passes
- `SUPERSEDED-BY-X` (related to Probes 4/5): a workflow exists, but an incumbent primitive already owns it or was selected by prior verdict (e.g., cognee → Graphiti L3 verdict)
- `PLUGIN-NAMESPACE` (Probe 4): the artifact already exists in a loaded plugin namespace
- `MODE-HARNESS-SHAPE` (Probe 5): candidate requires an operating mode or harness assumption sss does not satisfy

**Empirical evidence**: n=1 fresh self-observed (bytebase/dbhub REJECT-FOR-FIT 0.85, 2026-05-03 — Agent task output records "no application-DB query workflow"; existing DB-adjacent MCPs cover all live use cases) + n=1 retro corroboration (apollo-graphql per parent `Z:/claude/.claude/projects/Z--claude/memory/reference_mcp_disabled_rationale.md:20`). 4 historical disabled MCPs (claude-context/grafana/opik/local-judge) NOT counted under this Probe — they are CREDENTIAL-GATED-PENDING / A/B-PENDING / SUPERSEDED-BY-X / sss-config-drift distinct classes per the discriminator above.

**Wave 27 caveat (2026-05-05)**: dbhub may be DEMAND-CREATES-NEW-WORKFLOW.b for audit-log SQL analytics, but ONLY with an explicit JSONL → SQLite/Postgres ETL path. DBHub does not currently provide direct JSONL querying. The Wave 27 .b codification specifically opens a pilot pathway IF the operator commits to building the ETL surface — without it, dbhub still falls under Probe 7.a REJECT-FOR-FIT.

Anti-pattern: adopting a primitive because it's SOTA-valid + plugin-namespace-clean + license-compatible WITHOUT identifying a single sss workflow that consumes it. Result is MCP slot occupation without operational driver — historically the disabled-MCP cohort pattern (n=6 in `manifests/services.yaml:80-98` plus `.claude/settings.json:217` `disabledMcpjsonServers`).

Anti-pattern (Probe 7.b bypass — Wave 27 codification): claiming `.b` STUDY-PILOT eligibility for a candidate that fails any earlier Axis-4 blocker (PLUGIN-NAMESPACE / MODE-HARNESS-SHAPE / SUPERSEDED-BY-X / Probe 6 / META-HARNESS competing-framework shape per `docs/verified-avoid.md` Cohort 1). The `.b` escape hatch is admissible ONLY for structurally sound primitives — see Hard precondition above. Without the precondition gate, `.b` becomes a bypass for any attractive new primitive that duplicates `team-lead` / 12-agent fleet / T1-T7 lifecycle / etc.

### Codex-rescue verdict provenance: wrapper-only vs BRIDGE-MODE (Wave 17 fire-36 codification per real GPT-5.5 codex T1 fire-33 prescribed_edit #3)

Codex-rescue dispatches have TWO distinct execution modes that need explicit disclosure per `cross-model-consensus.md §Env-funneled subagent stand-in disclosure mandate`:

1. **codex-rescue Sonnet-wrapper-only**: agent runs entirely under `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=claude-sonnet-4-6` per `CLAUDE.local.md` ENV (f); does NOT bridge to codex CLI. Verdict origin = Sonnet stand-in. **Cross-model consensus NOT satisfied.** Requires 2nd-stage validation per FM-09 specialization above.

2. **codex-rescue BRIDGE-MODE**: agent's Sonnet wrapper invokes real GPT-5.5 via `codex exec --ephemeral -p deep-review-exec` subprocess (background or foreground+tee). Verdict origin = real GPT-5.5 codex CLI. **Cross-model consensus IS satisfied** for the codex-CLI verdict portion.

#### Disclosure shape (mandatory per fire-24 mandate)

- **Wrapper-only**: `STAND-IN per CLAUDE.local.md ENV (f); codex CLI not invoked; verdict is Sonnet stand-in only — orchestrator must file 2nd-stage validation if recommending ADOPT/STUDY`
- **BRIDGE-MODE**: `BRIDGE-MODE: codex-rescue Sonnet wrapper invoking real GPT-5.5 via codex CLI subprocess at <task-id>; verdict origin = codex CLI; cross-model gate satisfied`

#### IMPORTANT: BRIDGE-MODE does NOT waive Probe 4-7 harness-fit checks

Even in BRIDGE-MODE, codex-rescue STILL has the FM-09 abstract-pattern-reasoning blind-spot for plugin-namespace + mode-harness-shape + direct-file-blockers + demand-absence probes. The model-asymmetry is satisfied; the harness-fit-domain-knowledge gap is NOT. For ADOPT-NOW / STUDY-PILOT recommendations, ALWAYS run the full 6-probe DAG (Probes 1-7) regardless of dispatch mode.

#### Empirical evidence

- BRIDGE-MODE precedent: Wave 17 fire-25C codex-rescue dispatch successfully bridged to bg job `bc0yt5r9i` per `tmp/wave17-fire25-token-eff-synthesis-2026-05-04.md:88` (fire-25C verdict origin = real GPT-5.5)
- Wrapper-only precedent: pre-fire-25C codex-rescue dispatches in earlier sessions (FM-09 n=5 ladder) — all required 2nd-stage validation per the original FM-09 specialization above

### Codex-rescue blind-spot specialization (FM-09 promotion iter-67)

n=5 same-arc 2026-05-02 evidence ladder establishes that **codex-rescue agent (GPT-5.5 deep-dive variant) systematically returns ADOPT-NOW / STUDY-PILOT verdicts WITHOUT running Probe 4 / Probe 5 / Probe 6**. Pattern is agent-architecture-specific, not user-error: codex-rescue specializes in abstract-pattern reasoning (Axis 1+2+3 SOTA classification — T1 sources / T2 practitioners / Axis 3 stability) NOT repo-local-inventory reasoning (Axis 4 harness-fit). Different reasoning surfaces; codex-rescue's strength is the source of its blind-spot.

**The 2-stage validation contract** (n=5 base rate validated 100% on observable cases):

1. **Receive codex-rescue ADOPT-NOW / STUDY-PILOT verdict** on awesome-agentic-patterns / SOTA-catalog abstract-pattern candidates
2. **Spawn 2nd-stage harness-fit-aware agent** (`sota-researcher` / `architect` / `Explore` — **NOT codex-rescue intermediary** which has the same blind-spot)
3. **2nd-stage brief MUST INCLUDE explicit Probe 4 + Probe 5 + Probe 6 + Probe 7 mandate** (Probe 7 added 2026-05-03; historical FM-09 ladder at L103-127 was based on Probe 4-6 only — NOT retroactively reclassified per `port-note-discipline.md §6` anti-pattern "Do not rewrite historical commit bodies/snapshots") with file:line cite scaffold per `feedback_agent_team_brief_must_cite_sota_repos_at_file_line_2026_05_02.md`
4. **2nd-stage verdict OVERRIDES first-stage** if any probe fails — no consensus arbitration; harness-fit is veto authority over SOTA-classification
5. **HONEST-NON-FINDING is high-value output** per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` — REJECT-FOR-FIT verdicts save downstream T1+T2+ship+revert cycles
6. **2nd-stage FM-17 BLOCK protection** (per Wave 48 Agent A finding F-4 conf=0.85): if 2nd-stage harness-fit agent fails to return a verdict (FM-17 class — 429 / timeout / wedge / stream-watchdog stall): treat as BLOCK — first-stage codex-rescue ADOPT-NOW verdict CANNOT ship without completed 2nd-stage validation. Document under `.claude/rules/fm17-subagent-fleet-depletion.md` recovery in close-synthesis. Re-dispatch 2nd-stage when pool available OR perform orchestrator-direct Probe DAG 1-7 manual run. NEVER allow first-stage ADOPT to propagate as ship-decision under 2nd-stage FM-17 loss.

**Anti-pattern**: re-fire codex-rescue with "also run Probe 4+5+6" — codex-rescue's strength is abstract-pattern reasoning depth; doubling its scope dilutes that depth without compensating for the architectural blind-spot. **Spawn 2nd-stage agent in parallel; do NOT serialize codex-rescue with itself.**

**FM-09 evidence ladder (n=5 same-arc 2026-05-02)**:

| # | Ship | codex-rescue verdict | 2nd-stage agent | 2nd-stage verdict | Probe miss |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | F (Stop Hook Auto-Continue) | ADOPT-NOW conf=0.94 | sota-researcher | REJECT-FOR-FIT | Probe 4 plugin-namespace (3 Stop hooks + 2 ECC skills already cover) |
| 2 | G (Cross-Cycle Consensus Relay) | ADOPT-NOW conf=0.92 | architect | REJECT-FOR-FIT | Probe 4 + Probe 5 (5+ existing surfaces decompose CCCR; convergence-gate ALL 4 axes fail) |
| 3 | H (Memory Synthesis from Logs) | ADOPT-NOW conf=0.89 | (3-layer wrapper-chain opacity) | Pattern B HNF | wrapper-chain observability gap → recovery: skip codex-rescue intermediary |
| 4 | Memory/RAG audit (openviking) | sota-researcher STUDY-PILOT 0.78 | architect direct dispatch | REJECT-FOR-FIT 0.92 | Probe 6 LICENSE/badge (AGPLv3 license blocker) |
| 5 | D (ast-grep MCP) | sota-researcher STUDY-PILOT 0.85 | orchestrator pre-flight Probe 5b | REJECT-FOR-FIT (phantom package) | Probe 6 npm-registry direct-existence (phantom @anthropic/mcp-ast-grep) |

Base rate: 5/5 observable cases overridden by 2nd-stage harness-fit-aware audit (Ship H opaque variant counts as recovery-required, not observable disagreement). FM-09 base rate = 100% — codex-rescue ADOPT-NOW / STUDY-PILOT on abstract patterns SHOULD always be re-validated by 2-stage dispatch.

**Mechanics source memory**: `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota-installed/memory/feedback_fm09_codex_rescue_skipped_probe4_n2_2026_05_02.md` (full mechanics + iter-66 audit-trail correction).

## Anti-patterns

- **Trusting agent ranking verbatim without 4-axis probe** — refuted by n=5 same-arc REJECTs. The agent's PASS verdict is necessary but NOT sufficient.
- **Skipping probe 4 because "the upstream repo is local"** — local clone existence ≠ ship-readiness. ECC plugin EXPOSES many ECC artifacts as plugin-loaded skills; vendoring them again duplicates.
- **Promoting at n=1 without classifying the failure into one of the 7 sub-classes** — refuted by `codification-threshold.md` cycle-322 jurisdiction; class identification is what makes promotion durable.
- **Applying Axis 4 BEFORE Axis 1+2+3** — the order matters: SOTA classification is necessary upstream context; harness-fit is the downstream filter. A non-SOTA candidate doesn't need Axis 4 (already REJECTED).

Successor trigger: **An 8th distinct sub-class surfaces** (currently 7 classes; promote to skill-layer at sub-class n=5+ instances per class — Probe 5 currently at n=2 / Probe 6 at n=2 / Probe 7 at n=2, monitor for additional same-class instances)
- ECC plugin or codex plugin SHIPS a new namespace exposing artifacts claude-sota previously vendored — cleanup retroactive duplicates
- A NEW agent type (e.g. fresh discovery wave Z) demonstrates same gap → strengthens promotion
- Anthropic CC ships first-party plugin-namespace-aware vendor primitive (would obviate this rule's manual probe)

# Dogfood failure: v0.1 of this rule was drafted BEFORE T1 — exactly the violation the rule codifies. codex-t1-gate WARN fired correctly. Retroactive T1 fired post-draft (verdict at .claude/state/codex_consult_cross_model_consensus_design_OUT.txt — NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.88, 5 findings: 2 high + 3 medium). v0.2 (this version) integrates the fix-forward in a single iter per closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md NEEDS-REVISION disposition. n=2 evidence for the T1-mandatory rule (n=1 was iter-1 sota-loop-discipline.md v0.1 same dogfood pattern, deferred to reports/ at REJECT). The recovery path here is fix-forward-before-first-commit, NOT REVERT-AND-REMOVE — the file was never committed without T1 verdict on file.

Single-model design produces silent drift. Opus 4.7 drafting a rule looks coherent to Opus reading it back; codex GPT-5.5 reading the same rule independently catches mismatches Opus's self-audit misses (cite line-range errors, terminology drift across sibling rules, internal-vs-external state conflation, narrative-vs-protocol confusion). The cross-model consensus contract is: **Opus 4.7 designs + drafts → GPT-5.5 codex audits at lifecycle touchpoints (T1-T7) → verdict drives ship / fix-forward / STOP**. No commit lands on a design surface without codex's verdict on file.

This rule codifies the contract. It interlocks with `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` (verdict trajectory disposition), `synthesis-layer-verify.md` (verdict report shape), `evidence-policy.md` (verdict marker discipline), and `audit-action-loop.md` (post-audit drift closure).

### Discipline (Pattern A from real GPT-5.5 codex T1 fire-28)

- A SECTION that introduces or modifies a normative mechanism MUST add a row to this Map.
- A SECTION that only inherits / cross-refs / lists anti-patterns MAY omit (no new policy = no new authority claim).
- COMPOSED claims MUST follow the mechanical source-class reduction lattice at `.claude/rules/citation-discipline.md` rule #8 (UPGRADED at Wave 17 fire-36 per real GPT-5.5 codex T1 fire-33 NEEDS-REVISION on T2 b8fa7dd1 finding "weakest-source rule not computable"). Concretely: declare `constituents=[<class1@cite1>, ...]` + `effective_tier=MIN_PRECEDENCE(constituents)`. Authority Map rows above DECLARE constituents (per row) + EFFECTIVE class is computed per the lattice — this section does NOT carry a softer duplicate of the reduction rule.
- This Map is the BODY-level cite-trail discipline that complements frontmatter TIER-1 anchors at lines 9-11.

## The contract (cardinal rule)

For every Edit / Write / MultiEdit on a design surface (gate is default-in-scope and skips only `_UNIVERSAL_EXCLUSIONS` per `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:23-28,104-108,232,1717-1719`; common in-scope: rules, agents, commands, skills, settings, `.claude/schemas/*.json`, CLAUDE*.md, .mcp.json, PINS.json — NOT exhaustive):

1. **Opus drafts**. Synthesize from primary sources (Z:/repos/deps file:line, official docs, prior in-repo rules). Write the consult prompt that frames the design.
2. **Codex audits at T1**. Fire `codex exec --ephemeral -p deep-review-exec --output-last-message .claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt < .claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>.txt` BEFORE writing the design surface (or at minimum, in parallel — but with the file unstaged until verdict integrates). **Canonical pair-basename rule**: prompt path is `.claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>.txt`; verdict path is `.claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt`. The codex_t1_consult_gate `_consult_pairs()` algorithm strips `_OUT` from output basename and looks for prompt at exact stem — `codex_consult_<topic>_PROMPT.txt` does NOT pair with `codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt`. Operator-side fix for FM-08 codex-t1-gate WARN false-positive class (n=14+ session arc 2026-04-29→05-02 caused by my prompts living in `tmp/` instead of `.claude/state/<topic>.txt`).
3. **Verdict integrates**. Apply first-round dispositions from `§Verdict shapes + closed-loop integration`: APPROVE → ship; NEEDS-REVISION → apply prescribed edits as Pattern A fix-forward per `.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` (max 1 fix-forward iter per round) and re-fire T1 on the patched design surface; REJECT → STOP, report, and do not commit (remove untracked file before commit).
4. **Verdict reading: EOF FIRST**. Per `feedback_codex_t1_verdict_reading_discipline.md` (TIER-3 local provenance) — codex deep-review-exec may echo verdict templates near the start and emit the real verdict at EOF; `wc -l <file>` first; `grep -nE "VERDICT:|^F-[0-9]|conf=|confidence|APPROVE|NEEDS-REVISION|REJECT|severity (P[0-3])" <file> | tail -20`; then `tail -200 <file>` to confirm the final verdict block at EOF.
5. **Codex audits at T2/T3 (when wired)**. T2 reviews the working tree before commit; T3 fires automatically post-commit.
6. **Ship lands** with the codex verdict file referenced in commit body (`.claude/state/codex_consult_*_OUT.txt` for T1; `.claude/state/codex_review_HEAD_<sha>.txt` for T3 when wired).

The contract is non-negotiable. Recovery paths by violation timing:

- **Pre-commit T1 miss** (file untracked, no commit yet — e.g. v0.1 of this rule): retroactive T1 + single fix-forward if verdict permits. NEEDS-REVISION → fix-forward; APPROVE → ship; REJECT → STOP and remove the file before any commit.
- **Post-commit single-model design surface** (commit landed without T1 verdict on file): per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` §Disposition signal severity gate — Outcome B REVERT-AND-REMOVE on unresolved high/critical, Outcome C MANUAL-OVERRIDE on medium-with-strong-mitigation.

The first path (pre-commit miss) is the recovery path used by this very rule: v0.1 was untracked when the dogfood failure surfaced, retroactive T1 ran, NEEDS-REVISION verdict integrates here in v0.2 — no commit ever landed without T1 verdict on file.

**Discipline-side / hook-side relationship**: PreToolUse `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:232,1717-1719` targets Edit/Write/MultiEdit; when T1 is absent, the default path warns (non-blocking), while `CODEX_T1_GATE_STRICT=1` with auto-spawn enabled routes through AUTO-T1 at `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:1847-1868` and `_auto_spawn_codex_t1` at `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:1238-1455`. This `§The contract` body is the DISCIPLINE-side complement to hook-side enforcement — agent body sections point HERE rather than duplicate mechanics. cf. this file's Dogfood failure note at `cross-model-consensus.md:14` where the rule itself was caught by the gate it codifies before first commit.

Profile column updated iter-93 2026-05-02 from `deep-review` (stale — T6 needs danger-full-access for full-session-end audit) to `deep-review-exec`. |
