Until P3/P5 ports land repo-local hooks/commands, T1 is the load-bearing touchpoint and substitutes for T3-T6. The contract still holds; the wire is partially manual.

## Profile selection rule

Codex profile presets live at `Z:/claude-sota-installed/.codex/config.toml` (the local repo-resident `.codex/` is the authoritative config; the parent cache path `Z:/claude/.claude/plugins/cache/openai-codex/codex/1.0.4/` is TIER-3 operational provenance only). Three canonical profiles cover the T1-T7 lifecycle (table updated iter-93 2026-05-02 per codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.91 verdict at `.claude/state/codex_consult_iter93_t2_exec_profile_addition_OUT.txt` Q4 prescription):

- **`deep-review-exec`**: cloud `gpt-5.5` xhigh-effort + danger-full-access sandbox + service_tier=fast + verbosity=high. Used by T1 pre-edit consult hook (`codex_t1_consult_gate.py`), T3 postcommit hook (`codex_postcommit_review.py:599` per codex T1 verdict at `codex_consult_iter93_*_OUT.txt` Q6 P3), T4 post-push hook (`codex_prepush_review.py:593`), and T6 stop-gate (`codex_stop_review_gate.py`). Token budget ~5K-10K per invocation.
- **`deep-review`**: cloud `gpt-5.5` xhigh-effort + read-only sandbox + service_tier=fast + verbosity=high. Used by T5 manual `/plan-codex-review` slash command (read-only suffices for prompt-context bounded reads). Token budget ~5K-10K per invocation.
- **`t2-exec`** (added iter-93 2026-05-02): cloud `gpt-5.5` HIGH-effort (NOT xhigh — T2 mechanical scope) + read-only sandbox + service_tier=fast + verbosity=medium. Used for **operator-side custom-prompt T2 reviews** ONLY: `codex exec --ephemeral -p t2-exec < .claude/state/codex_t2_<topic>.txt > .claude/state/codex_t2_<topic>_OUT.txt 2>&1`. Token budget ~1K-3K per invocation.

**Important asymmetry — T2 commit-time runtime hook does NOT use `-p`**: the runtime pre-commit gate `codex_t2_pre_commit_gate.py:424` uses `codex exec review --uncommitted` which REJECTS `-p` per `codex-cli-flag-positioning.md` matrix row 1. The hook routes via codex CLI's default profile. The `t2-exec` profile is exclusively for operator-side standalone T2 fires with custom prompts (e.g., this rule's own iter-93 retroactive T1 + sister-update). Per codex T1 Q4 prescription verbatim: "Do not codify `t2-exec` as a hook T2 profile: `codex exec review --uncommitted` rejects `-p`, so add `t2-exec` as an operator-side custom-prompt T2 convention only".

**Legacy / non-T1-T7 profiles** (kept in config.toml for batch / classification workloads, NOT for verdict-composition T2):
- **`headless-exec`** (gemma4:vision local Ollama): RETIRED for operator-side T2 custom-prompt reviews per n=2 same-arc 180s timeouts at iter-92 fires `bfawoapn0` + `bl47e7iin` (commit `f3c1e57` body Pattern B trail). Local 7B-class VLM cannot compose verdict block in 180s budget for natural-language T2 prompts. Still correct for pure classification / image / multimodal tasks where Ollama VLM excels. Memory: `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota-installed/memory/feedback_headless_exec_t2_profile_drift_2026_05_02.md`.
- **`local`** / **`review`** / **`strict`**: see `.codex/config.toml:[profiles.X]` blocks for use cases.

Pick by surface complexity and depth-of-reasoning needed, not by file count. A 21-line evidence-policy port can need `deep-review-exec` if the cite-class adjudication is non-trivial; a 500-line working-tree diff can use `t2-exec` if the changes are mechanical and need cloud-fast-tier verdict latency.

## Timeout + failover policy

- T1 pre-edit consult: **240s narrow** (3-axis prompt) / **900s deep** (full design audit). Narrow is default; escalate to deep only when the design touches multiple sibling rules or introduces a new cite-class.
- T2/T3/T4: **120-180s** sufficient for `--uncommitted` or postcommit-diff scope.
- T5 plan-stage: **240s**.
- T6 stop-gate: **900s** (covers full session-end review).

**On timeout**: fail-closed → mine the partial trace for embedded evidence, document the timeout in the commit body (`T1 INCONCLUSIVE-TIMEOUT after 240s — partial trace mined, no NEW-CLASS findings emerged`), proceed only if no high-severity findings surfaced from the partial output. `feedback_codex_t1_timeout_extract_partial_evidence.md` is TIER-3 local pattern provenance (FORWARD-REF — pending memory-port to claude-sota).

**On codex unavailable** (`codex exec` returns cmd-not-found / 3 consecutive failures): the documented `local-judge` MCP fallback is currently UNAVAILABLE — `local-judge` is in `.claude/settings.json:disabledMcpjsonServers` as of 2026-05-03, so `mcp__local-judge__route` + `invoke_judge` will return tool-not-found. Recovery options:

- **(a) DEFAULT — Queue + retry**: queue T1 unavailable + codex re-review when codex CLI recovers (preserves cross-model consensus invariant; preferred for non-urgent design reviews). **Maximum queue window** (per Wave 48 Agent A finding F-5 conf=0.80): 4h for medium-risk ships; 24h for low-risk ships. If codex unavailable beyond window: high-risk ships BLOCK until recovery; medium-risk evaluate option (b) with fresh smoke-probe AND explicit `[OVERRIDE]` marker; low-risk proceed with Pattern B HNF disposition per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md`.
- **(b) FALLBACK — Direct Ollama HTTP bridge** at `http://127.0.0.1:11700/api/generate` against `qwen3.6:judge` model. Backend liveness MEASURED [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via curl `/api/version` returning `{"version":"0.22.1"}` + codex T1 secondary probe `/api/tags` listing `qwen3.6:judge` + `gemma4:vision`].

> **⚠️ CAUTION — Option (b) `/api/generate` endpoint is UNCERTIFIED.** Neither the `/api/generate` endpoint nor the `qwen3.6:judge` model's verdict-shape compatibility has been smoke-tested for cross-model T1 verdict-composition. **First-use of option (b) MUST probe before trusting verdicts**: send a minimal payload with `"stream": false` set explicitly (e.g., `{"model": "qwen3.6:judge", "prompt": "APPROVE, NEEDS-REVISION, or REJECT?", "stream": false}` — without `"stream": false`, Ollama `/api/generate` defaults to NDJSON streaming and the JSON-shape parse below will false-negative even when endpoint is healthy) + verify `{"response": "..."}` JSON shape returns within 60s + verify model emits APPROVE / NEEDS-REVISION / REJECT structured output as expected by `cross-model-consensus.md §Verdict shapes`. If smoke-probe fails, fall back to option (a) deferred-queue rather than trust an uncertified verdict.

Mark commit body — option (a): `T1 via option-a-deferred — codex re-review queued`; option (b) **DOWNGRADED-MODE** (per Wave 48 Agent A finding F-2 P1 conf=0.88): `T1 DOWNGRADED: qwen3.6:judge via Ollama (UNCERTIFIED — cross-model gate NOT satisfied; GPT-5.5 unavailable); ship under Outcome C MANUAL-OVERRIDE per closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md with explicit [OVERRIDE] marker; codex re-review queued when GPT-5.5 available`. Note: option (b) is renamed FALLBACK → **DOWNGRADED-MODE** to signal trust-boundary change at label level.

## Env-funneled subagent stand-in disclosure mandate (Wave 17 fire-24 codification per E2 audit P1-A)

# Reference: TIER-2 `Z:/claude-sota-installed/CLAUDE.local.md` ENV block (f) — `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=claude-sonnet-4-6` (Anthropic Max Opus depletion fallback, deployed 2026-04-29)
# Reference: TIER-2 `Z:/claude-sota-installed/tmp/wave17-E2-arc-level-review-2026-05-04.md §5` (P1-A finding: A3+C2+B2+D2+E2 ran as Sonnet stand-ins; true GPT-5.5 penetration ~25% not asserted 75%)
# Reference: TIER-2 `Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/rules/named-failure-modes.md` FM-17 row (subagent fleet-depletion final-return-loss; pool-funneling parent class)
# Reference: TIER-2 `Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/agents/gpt5-reviewer.md` frontmatter — fail-closed contract: "Codex unavailable is never a SKIP condition; invoke this agent and return VERDICT: BLOCK"

When `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` is set to a model OTHER than the canonical gpt5-reviewer / gpt5-archaeologist target (currently `gpt-5.5` via codex CLI), the subagent runs as a **same-model stand-in** rather than a true cross-model voice. The cross-model consensus invariant — that codex GPT-5.5 catches what Opus/Sonnet self-audit miss — does NOT structurally hold under stand-in dispatch. The dispatch surface looks identical to the orchestrator (same agent name, same prompt template, same return shape) but the model-asymmetry that makes the gate meaningful is silently absent.

### The mandate

A `gpt5-reviewer` / `gpt5-archaeologist` dispatch under env-funneling MUST do ONE of:

1. **(Default)** Return `VERDICT: BLOCK` per the agent's fail-closed contract at `.claude/agents/gpt5-reviewer.md` frontmatter. Stand-in dispatch does NOT satisfy the cross-model gate; BLOCK is the safe action under structural same-model degradation.
2. **(Explicit-disclosure)** Return APPROVE / NEEDS-REVISION / REJECT WITH explicit `STAND-IN-NOTICE: agent ran under CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=<model> stand-in per CLAUDE.local.md ENV block (f); cross-model gate NOT structurally satisfied for this dispatch` in the verdict body's first 5 lines. The orchestrator then bears the integration decision: re-fire via real codex CLI, accept stand-in verdict with documented gate-bypass rationale, or REVERT-AND-REMOVE per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` Outcome B.
3. **(Operator escalation)** If codex CLI is unreachable (FM-03 D1 transport): return BLOCK + queue-and-retry per the existing "On codex unavailable" recovery options at §"Timeout + failover policy" above.

### Orchestrator integration discipline

When integrating a STAND-IN-NOTICE verdict into a composite synthesis (e.g., Wave fan-out close-synthesis.md), the orchestrator MUST:

1. **Surface the stand-in penetration rate** in the close-synthesis verdict body — e.g., "5/9 dispatches ran as Sonnet stand-in (~56%); cross-model coverage is ~44% of nominal; treat composite verdict accordingly".
2. **Reject blanket "N% GPT-5.5 dispatched" claims** without per-dispatch classification (true GPT-5.5 via `codex exec` CLI vs Sonnet stand-in via Anthropic SDK vs codex-rescue wrapper-around-codex-CLI). This was the empirical class-error caught in Wave 17 E2 review §5 — claimed 75% GPT-5.5 vs measured ~25%.
3. **Cite the stand-in classification** in commit body when the ship integrates a stand-in verdict (e.g., `T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.83 [STAND-IN per CLAUDE.local.md ENV (f); see tmp/wave17-E2-arc-level-review-2026-05-04.md §5]`). Per `evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay corollary + the MUST-USE `[VERIFIED via <path>]` extension at §"Evidence marker discipline" above.

### Empirical evidence (n=5 same-arc Wave 17 2026-05-04)

| # | Wave | Agent | Self-declared stand-in evidence | Cross-model gate satisfied? |
|---|------|-------|--------------------------------|------------------------------|
| 1 | Wave 17 fire-16 A3 | gpt5-reviewer | YES — `tmp/wave17-A3-adversarial-review-2026-05-04.md:4` "agent: gpt5-reviewer (claude-sonnet-4-6 stand-in per FM-17 pool funneling)" | NO |
| 2 | Wave 17 fire-16 C2 | gpt5-reviewer | YES — `tmp/wave17-C2-adversarial-fire16-cascade-2026-05-04.md:5` "agent: gpt5-reviewer (claude-sonnet-4-6 proxy — codex CLI unavailable; fail-closed per agent SYSTEM PROMPT contract)" | NO |
| 3 | Wave 17 fire-20 B2 | gpt5-archaeologist | INFERRED — FM-19 truncated, but class-wrapper inherits same env-funneling | NO (assumed stand-in by class inheritance) |
| 4 | Wave 17 fire-20 D2 | gpt5-archaeologist | INFERRED — FM-19 truncated, but class-wrapper inherits same env-funneling | NO (assumed stand-in by class inheritance) |
| 5 | Wave 17 E2 audit | gpt5-reviewer | YES — `tmp/wave17-E2-arc-level-review-2026-05-04.md:4` "agent: gpt5-reviewer (claude-sonnet-4-6 proxy — codex CLI unavailable per readonly Bash guard FM-19)" | NO |

True GPT-5.5 penetration ~25% (5 codex CLI jobs out of ~20 total dispatches per E2 §5 fleet dispatch classification) — NOT 75% as previously claimed. The user directive "USE GPT5.5 AGGRESSIVELY" has NOT been operationally satisfied at the gpt5-* subagent layer; it IS satisfied at the codex CLI layer when foreground+tee fires successfully.

### Recovery actions for orchestrator

Per the n=5 evidence ladder, when the orchestrator detects stand-in pattern:

- **Compose**: orchestrator-side prompt to gpt5-reviewer/gpt5-archaeologist MUST include `IF YOUR FRONTMATTER MODEL ≠ gpt-5.5 OR YOUR DISPATCH RUNS UNDER CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=<not-gpt-5.5>: return VERDICT: BLOCK + STAND-IN-NOTICE per cross-model-consensus.md §Env-funneled subagent stand-in disclosure mandate.` This forces explicit disclosure rather than relying on agent self-awareness.
- **Audit**: at iter-close synthesis, orchestrator MUST classify each gpt5-* dispatch into {true-GPT-5.5-via-codex-CLI / Sonnet-stand-in-via-SDK / codex-rescue-wrapper-of-codex-CLI / unknown-FM-19-truncated} and surface penetration rate.
- **Remediate**: when penetration <50%, recommend operator-side `unset CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` or set `=gpt-5.5` (where SDK supports it) before re-firing the wave.

### Update triggers

Re-evaluate this section when:
- `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` env is unset or set to a model OTHER than `claude-sonnet-4-6` (Anthropic Max Opus pool restored OR pool-funnel target changes) — flip mandate from MUST-BLOCK-by-default to "warn if env-funneled" since stand-in becomes exceptional rather than default
- Anthropic CC ships a per-dispatch model attestation primitive (e.g., `agent_dispatch_actual_model` hook field, `subagent_provenance` system-reminder) that surfaces stand-in status structurally rather than via agent self-declaration
- A 6th cross-arc instance surfaces (would expand the n=5 evidence ladder; promotion-ladder for sub-rule extraction at n=6+)
- A subagent class ships that has NO equivalent codex CLI tool path (would require expanding the discipline beyond gpt5-reviewer / gpt5-archaeologist to include the new class)

### Related rules + cross-references

- `named-failure-modes.md` FM-17 — subagent fleet-depletion final-return-loss; this section's stand-in class is the PEER mechanism (env-funneling rather than depletion-during-dispatch)
- `agent-harness-fit-verification.md` Probe 5 mode-harness-shape — when an agent's required model is unavailable, that's a Probe-5-class mismatch
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §SubagentStop transcript-mining` — orchestrator-side audit surface that COULD detect stand-in via transcript model field if Anthropic CC exposes it
- `evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay — `[VERIFIED via codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.83]` decays IF the dispatch was stand-in; orchestrator must qualify with `[STAND-IN per ENV (f)]` marker

## Verdict shapes + closed-loop integration

Codex T1 verdicts come in 3 shapes. **First-round T1 verdict disposition is distinct from closed-loop arc disposition** — closed-loop outcomes (A/B/C) only invoke when the SAME logical surface re-fires across rounds:

| Verdict | First-round disposition | Re-fire (closed-loop arc) |
|---|---|---|
| **APPROVE** | Ship as-designed (commit lands with verdict file referenced) | n/a — first-round APPROVE never invokes closed-loop |
| **NEEDS-REVISION** | Apply findings, single fix-forward (max 1 per iter per `.claude/rules/sota-loop-discipline.md` §G5 — pending P2.h port). Re-fire codex T1 on the patched file | If re-fire NEEDS-REVISION → Outcome A ACCEPT-WITH-DOC (cap-exit at iter-5 per §closed-loop §G5) |
| **REJECT** | STOP, do not commit; if file is untracked remove it; escalate | If re-fire still REJECT → Outcome B REVERT-AND-REMOVE (per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` §Disposition signal severity-gate hard-deny on unresolved high/critical) |

Confidence trajectory is the load-bearing signal across re-fires. Track across rounds, not just final round. Manual override (Outcome C MANUAL-OVERRIDE) requires explicit `[OVERRIDE]` marker per `evidence-policy.md` and applies only to medium-severity findings with strong mitigation documented in commit body.

## Severity taxonomy mapping (codex P0-P3 ↔ industry Critical/Important/Minor)

# Reference: Z:/repos/deps/superpowers/skills/requesting-code-review/code-reviewer.md:64-93 @ HEAD e7a2d16476bf042e9add4699c9d018a90f86e4a6 [VERIFIED 2026-04-30 via wave-3 source-mining methodology-audit-PASS at docs/sota-grand-catalog/wave-3-2026-04-30/source-mining/superpowers-deep-read.md (commit 11bf5eb)]

Per superpowers (named-T2 author obra; 3-axis convergence-gate PASS per wave-3 source-mining), industry-standard code-review severity vocabulary maps to sss codex P0/P1/P2/P3 numeric scale. Use this mapping in T1/T2/T3 verdict reports for cross-tool portability:

| Codex severity | Industry vocab | Action threshold | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P0** (CRITICAL) | **Critical** | STOP; do not commit; revert if landed | Security vulnerability; data loss; default-allow on destructive Bash; secret leak |
| **P1** (HIGH) | **Important** | Block commit; require fix-forward before merge | Logic bug in load-bearing path; broken contract; missing test for DENY-emitting hook |
| **P2** (MEDIUM) | **Minor (high)** | Apply fix-forward in same PR; not blocking | Suboptimal pattern; dead code; marker discipline gap; cite-tier downgrade |
| **P3** (LOW) | **Minor (low)** | Queue as separate ship; document in commit body | Stylistic; naming consistency; doc-only nits |

Source convention: superpowers `code-reviewer.md:64-93` uses Critical / Important / Minor as the 3-tier external-facing taxonomy; sss codex uses P0/P1/P2/P3 4-tier internal numeric scale. This mapping bridges the two so cross-tool review reports (e.g., when codex T2 verdict is consumed by external reviewers using superpowers-style rubric, or when sss reviews a PR authored against superpowers conventions) maintain semantic equivalence.

Cross-references:
- `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md §Disposition signal severity-gate` — hard-deny triggers on unresolved Critical/Important (P0/P1) findings
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` — orthogonal axis (severity classifies findings; subclaim-type classifies claim sources)
- `evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay — severity is also marker-decayable (P1 yesterday may be P0 today if upstream context changed)

## Risk-stratified verification gating

# Reference: `https://resources.anthropic.com/2026-agentic-coding-trends-report` (TIER-1 Anthropic; full PDF at `https://resources.anthropic.com/hubfs/2026%20Agentic%20Coding%20Trends%20Report.pdf`) [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via Wave-6 Agent M perplexity_research [17][31][48]] — Anthropic 2026 Agentic Coding Trends Report identifies risk-stratified gating as the SOTA pattern for AI-agent code change verification: low-risk = full-agent-delegate + post-commit review; medium-risk = pre-commit human review (agent implements, human approves); high-risk = design review BEFORE agent implements.
# Reference: Wave-6 Agent M Round-4 task design at `tmp/wave6-agent-m-sota-git-practice-2026-05-03.md:165-196` (ADOPT-NOW conf=0.92; cohort attribution C5 named-author Anthropic; Axis 1+2+3 PASS via STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS predicate satisfied — Anthropic-org-as-named-T2-equivalent maintainer).

Beyond the **finding-severity** taxonomy at §"Severity taxonomy mapping" above (which classifies what codex found AFTER work landed), the **task risk-class** axis classifies the change BEFORE work begins. Both axes are orthogonal — severity describes findings (P0/P1/P2/P3); risk-class describes tasks (low/medium/high). Both inform the T0-T7 lifecycle gate selection.

### The 3-tier risk taxonomy + T0-T7 gate mapping

| Risk class | Task signature | Verification gate | sss T0-T7 mapping |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Low-risk** | Reversible operations on isolated surfaces (tests / docs / non-cardinal-rule edits / bounded scripts ≤200 LOC where convergence is well-understood); single-axis-change with clear primary-source backing | Full-agent-delegate + **post-commit review only** (T3 PostToolUse `Bash(git commit *)`) | T1 SKIP per §"Mechanical-mirror exception" at `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md` IF predicates met; else light T1 + T3 verify |
| **Medium-risk** | Multi-file ships / new mechanics / behavioral changes / existing-surface edits with potential ripple; rule-layer or skill-layer additions ≤200 LOC | Pre-commit cross-model review (**T2 commit-time hook** at `Bash(git commit *)` PreToolUse — agent implements, codex GPT-5.5 reviews uncommitted tree before commit lands) | T1 pre-edit + T2 working-tree + T3 post-commit (full lifecycle) |
| **High-risk** | Cardinal-rule surface edits (CLAUDE.md / `.claude/settings.json` / `.mcp.json` / cardinal-rules-defining `.claude/rules/*.md`) / security-class operations / destructive Bash / >200 LOC architectural changes / new T-touchpoint or wedge-class introduction | **Design review BEFORE agent implements** (T0 candidate-list challenge + T1 deep-review-exec xhigh + T5 plan-stage; multi-cohort fan-out + Probe-DAG harness-fit verification BEFORE T1 fires) | T0 + T5 (pre-design) + T1 (pre-edit) + T2 (pre-commit) + T3 (post-commit) + T4 (post-push) + T6 (session-end) — full 7-touchpoint coverage |

### How to apply

1. **Classify task risk-class FIRST** (BEFORE writing T1 consult or scoping the ship):
   - Examine target file class (cardinal-rule surface? rule-layer? script? test?)
   - Examine LOC delta (≤200 LOC bounded vs >200 LOC architectural)
   - Examine reversibility (pure additive doc-only? behavioral mechanism change? security/destructive?)
   - Examine cite-class novelty (new TIER-1/TIER-2 source class introduced? new cite anchor pattern?)
2. **Select gate-set per risk-class** (per §The 3-tier risk taxonomy table above)
3. **Document risk-class in commit body** — include `risk-class: low | medium | high` line + 1-line classification rationale (e.g., `risk-class: medium — multi-file rule edit + new mechanics introduced; T1+T2+T3 lifecycle invoked`)
4. **Risk-class is REVISABLE pre-commit** — if T1 surfaces unanticipated cardinal-surface impact (e.g., the rule edit changes existing T-touchpoint semantics rather than adding a new section), upgrade to high-risk and fire T0+T5 retroactively before commit

### Anti-patterns

- **"All changes go through medium-risk gate"** — refuted by Anthropic taxonomy (Wave-6 Agent M source). Low-risk operations (test files, doc-only updates, additive bounded sections with no behavior change) are over-gated by medium-risk T1+T2 coverage; the medium-risk full lifecycle costs ~5-10 min per ship unnecessarily. Conversely, high-risk operations (CLAUDE.md cardinal-rule trim, settings.json env block changes, .mcp.json server addition) are under-gated by medium-risk-only T1+T2 — they need T0 candidate-list challenge + T5 plan-stage review BEFORE T1 fires.
- **Risk-class as severity proxy** — they're orthogonal axes. A low-risk ship can produce a P0 finding (e.g., test file accidentally exposes secret). A high-risk ship can produce only P3 findings (e.g., CLAUDE.md trim with all changes anticipated by design review). Don't conflate.
- **Static risk-class declaration without re-classification trigger** — if T1/T2 surfaces unanticipated cardinal-surface ripple, the original low-risk classification is invalid; re-classify to medium or high per the surface impacted, do NOT silently proceed under the original risk-class.

### Cross-references

- `severity-taxonomy-mapping` §above — orthogonal axis (severity describes findings AFTER; risk-class describes tasks BEFORE)
- `codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Mechanical-mirror exception` — operationalizes T1 SKIP for the low-risk + settled-source predicate combination
- `layered-gates-architecture.md §The 5 layers` — architectural authority for the gate layers each risk-class invokes
- `agent-harness-fit-verification.md §The 7 sub-classes` — Probe DAG that high-risk T0 candidate-list challenge invokes before T1 fires
- `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator` — UPSTREAM/OPERATIONAL/CATEGORY-CLAIM classification interacts with risk-class (CATEGORY-CLAIM ships often classify as low-risk; OPERATIONAL-CLAIM with new mechanics often medium-risk; UPSTREAM-CLAIM for new cited-dep adoption often high-risk)

## Verdict report shape (synthesis-layer-verify integration)

Codex T1 verdicts are subject to `synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Reporting categories — OVER / UNDER / HONEST-NON-FINDING:

- **OVER**: codex claims a finding the actual file doesn't contain (cite to non-existent line range, hallucinated structure). Recovery: verify against actual file, dispute the finding in fix-forward commit body, do not silently apply.
- **UNDER**: codex misses a real finding I'm aware of. Recovery: include the missed finding in fix-forward, document the omission. Doesn't invalidate the verdict but signals codex narrowness.
- **HONEST-NON-FINDING**: codex returns "no concerns on this axis" — that IS the deliverable for that axis, do not re-fire. The HNF axis is closed; iterate on remaining axes only.

Verify codex's findings against the actual file (cite line ranges, claimed wording) BEFORE applying. The synthesis-layer-verify rule applies recursively to codex's own output.

## Evidence marker discipline (evidence-policy integration)

Per `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md`, every claim in the commit body about codex's verdict MUST carry a marker:

- `[VERIFIED]` — verdict file read, line-cited, marker confirmed (e.g., "T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.88 [VERIFIED via .claude/state/codex_consult_*_OUT.txt]")
- `[VERIFIED yyyy-mm-dd]` — when freshness matters (codex re-verdict on a re-fire)
- `[INFERRED]` — claim about why codex flagged something, not codex's literal output
- `[UNKNOWN]` — codex didn't address (HNF on a different axis, but unknown on this one)

Don't paraphrase codex's verdict in commit body — quote the verdict's verbatim text where possible.

**MUST-USE `[VERIFIED via <path>]` convention extension** (F-C-02 forward-only codification iter-107): every commit body citing a T1/T2/T3/T4/T6 verdict with `confidence ≥ 0.85` (or any conf=N claim) MUST embed inline the verifying file path in `[VERIFIED via <path>]` form — e.g. `T1 NEEDS-REVISION conf=0.90 [VERIFIED via .claude/state/codex_consult_iter106_fm02_owner_propagation_OUT.txt]`. This closes the OPERATIONAL-CLAIM ambiguity per `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type discriminator`: without the path-anchored `[VERIFIED via <path>]` marker, readers cannot distinguish OPERATIONAL-CLAIM (verdict file actually contains the cited verdict) from CATEGORY-CLAIM (verdict file exists at the path but content unverified) without re-probing. **Forward-only application per `.claude/rules/port-note-discipline.md §6` anti-pattern "Do not rewrite historical commit bodies/snapshots to fix retroactive findings"** — historical iter-92→iter-101 commit bodies (8 of 9 commits in arc 2026-05-02 missing this marker per Wave 8 Agent C F-C-02 P2 finding at task `a89f4e9f163723385`) remain unmodified. iter-104 commit `9a22d27` exemplifies correct usage; iter-105 `011d885` + iter-106 `5747e10` carry forward; this rule's codification governs iter-107+ forward.

## Source-cite discipline for consult prompts

The consult prompt itself is **NOT a TIER-1 source** — it is an **agent transcript / evidence trail (TIER-3)** per cardinal rule #1 (CLAUDE.md:7 "NOT acceptable: agent-transcript derivatives"). The consult prompt's role is to FRAME design questions for codex; the prompt itself does not establish architectural authority. Each consult prompt MUST embed or point to TIER-1/TIER-2 primary citations for the design claims it asks codex to review.

**Acceptable cite classes inside the consult prompt** (per CLAUDE.md:7 cardinal rule #1):

- **TIER-1**: `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/file:line` at pinned SHA (e.g., `Z:/repos/deps/codex-plugin-cc/plugins/codex/scripts/codex-companion.mjs:N at SHA 807e03ac9d5aa23bc395fdec8c3767500a86b3cf` — verified content)
- **TIER-1**: Official docs URL with quote/anchor (e.g., `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#pretooluse`)
- **TIER-1**: `mcp__github__get_file_contents` result from STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS org per `.claude/rules/convergence-gate.md` Axis-3 5-band table
- **TIER-2**: `docs/<doc>.md` user-curated reference (e.g., `docs/codex-plugin-cc-sota-deep-dive.md`)

**NOT acceptable as authority** (TIER-3 evidence trails — they show the audit ran but they don't BACK new design claims):

- `.claude/state/codex_consult_*_OUT.txt` verdict files
- `.claude/state/codex_review_HEAD_<sha>.txt` postcommit/postpush verdict files
- `feedback_*.md` memory files (LOCAL CODIFIED LESSONS, not external SOTA)
- Commit SHAs (audit trail, not source authority)
- Prior in-repo rules that themselves only cite TIER-3 (transitive evidence-trail = still TIER-3)
- FORWARD-REF parent-ccc rules where the parent also lacks TIER-1 backing

Don't ask codex to verify a claim against a paraphrase. Quote the source verbatim or include the file path + line range.

[VERIFIED 2026-04-28 via codex T1 deep-review-exec REJECT 0.91 verdict F-001 P0 at `.claude/state/codex_consult_aggressive_audit_OUT.txt`; codex's prescription verbatim: "consult prompts/verdicts are TIER-3 evidence trails only. Require each consult prompt to embed or point to TIER-1/TIER-2 primary citations for the design claims it asks Codex to review."]

## Anti-patterns

- **Skip T1 because edit "looks mechanical"** — empirically refuted: every "mechanical" port edit this fire surfaced 1-6 codex findings on first T1. Mechanical surface ≠ codex-finds-nothing.
- **Skip T1 because the rule itself is about T1** (meta-recursive trap) — empirically caught at iter-9 (this rule's v0.1 was drafted before T1 fired; codex-t1-gate WARN fired correctly; retroactive T1 returned NEEDS-REVISION 0.88 with 5 findings). n=2 evidence with iter-1 sota-loop-discipline.md v0.1 (same dogfood pattern). The rule's authority does NOT exempt the rule from the rule. Especially when the rule IS the rule.
- **Trust codex paraphrase of source over actual file** — codex T1 can hallucinate cite line ranges; verify against actual file before applying findings (synthesis-layer-verify recursive application).
- **Layer-fix forever** — escalate to `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` Outcome B at round 5 OR confidence escalation between rounds.
- **Bundle multiple design surfaces in one T1** — split into per-surface consults; codex narrows better with focused scope (3-axis vs 7-axis from this fire's iter-1 sota-loop-discipline arc).
- **Skip codex when local-judge is "good enough"** — local-judge is failover, not substitute. Cross-model consensus requires GPT-5.5; same-architecture-judge consensus is single-model dressed up.
- **Ship without verdict file referenced in commit body** — the verdict file is the audit trail. Future cycles need to find it. Always cite `.claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt` or `.claude/state/codex_review_HEAD_<sha>.txt`.
- **Cite commit hashes in stable rule §Related cross-refs** — commit hashes belong in commit messages and provenance, not in cross-rule edges (per iter-8 codex finding).
- **Trust first T1 verdict hit instead of file END** — codex deep-review-exec outputs span 1k-10k lines and routinely echo the `VERDICT: APPROVE | NEEDS-REVISION | REJECT` template near the start before the actual investigation, then emit the real verdict at EOF. Discipline: `wc -l <file>` first; then `grep -nE "VERDICT:|^F-[0-9]|conf=|confidence|APPROVE|NEEDS-REVISION|REJECT|severity (P[0-3])" <file> | tail -20` (force last hits, not first); then `tail -200 <file>` and confirm the final verdict block is at EOF. Genuine-truncation branch: only call `INCONCLUSIVE-INCOMPLETE` when the last grep hits are prompt-echo / early-investigation AND `tail -200` ends mid-investigation with no final verdict — high line count alone does NOT imply completion. Empirically refuted on iter-17/18/20 completed T1 verdict files (n=3 verdicts at L4457/L10073/L1610 + EOF) plus iter-19 separate skipped-T1 propagation case — total n=4 affected commit-body claims, n=3 verified completed outputs. Failure class: `synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Reporting categories OVER. Operational home: this rule's verdict-reading + fail-closed protocol. Provenance: `feedback_codex_t1_verdict_reading_discipline.md` + iter-21 fix-forward.

## Sibling-rule integration

This rule sits above `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` (which it invokes for verdict trajectory disposition) and below `research-protocol.md` (which establishes the cite hierarchy and tool routing this rule depends on). Sibling integrations:

- `.claude/rules/research-protocol.md` — §Tool Routing table provides the MCP surface this rule uses (mcp__local-judge for failover **[CURRENTLY DISABLED — see `.claude/settings.json:disabledMcpjsonServers`; recovery alternatives at §"On codex unavailable" above]**, mcp__github for source verification). VERIFY corollaries 1+2 are pre-edit and post-edit observability that bookend this rule's during-edit consensus.
- `.claude/rules/closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` — re-fire arc disposition: **Outcome A ACCEPT-WITH-DOC** / **Outcome B REVERT-AND-REMOVE** / **Outcome C MANUAL-OVERRIDE**. First-round T1 verdict disposition is distinct (see §Verdict shapes); closed-loop outcomes only invoke on same-surface re-fire.
- `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` — **OVER / UNDER / HONEST-NON-FINDING** reporting categories apply to codex's verdict shape; the **Subclaim-type discriminator** (UPSTREAM-CLAIM / OPERATIONAL-CLAIM / CATEGORY-CLAIM) applies to claims under review.
- `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md` — markers required on verdict citations in commit body. The **Marker Decay corollary** applies to verdict citations across re-fires (a NEEDS-REVISION marker decays to UNKNOWN once the re-fire verdict lands, but only after the new verdict integrates).
- `.claude/rules/convergence-gate.md` — **Axis 3 5-band stability table** (including STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS) guides cite-class admissibility for adoption claims surfaced inside T1 consult prompts. Not a blanket T1 source-admissibility class — applies only when the consult is auditing an adoption decision.
- `.claude/rules/audit-action-loop.md` — when codex T1 surfaces SOTA-pin freshness drift, the **4-stage Wire / Surface / Close / Re-fire pattern** closes it.
- `.claude/rules/sota-pin-discipline.md` — codex T1 verdicts on **Tier-1 SOTA-pin claims** (model SHA / tool versions / MCP inventory / plugin versions / cited-repo HEADs) feed `chore(pins):` commit cycle. Tier-3/queued pins (network probes, eval scores) are out-of-scope for T1 cycle; they have their own audit cadence.

## Eval-case mandate (Phase 1 of Task #88 verdict→eval gap closure)

Per A4-retry codex GPT-5.5 adversarial verdict (Loop #4 2026-04-28; `docs/karpathy-llm-wiki-practice.md` `[loop4-iter1-N+2-a4-retry]` entry): claude-sota was verdict-driven (T1/T2 misses stored as prose only). The `evals/codex_miss_cases.jsonl` corpus + `evals/run_codex_miss_eval.py` runner (shipped Phase 1 atomic with this rule edit) converts each NEEDS-REVISION/REJECT into a replayable regression case.

**Mandate**: every codex T1/T2 NEEDS-REVISION verdict with `confidence ≥ 0.85` that drives a Pattern A FIX-FORWARD ship MUST either:

1. **Add or update an eval case** in `evals/codex_miss_cases.jsonl` capturing the regression invariant via the Phase 1 deterministic DSL (`contains_all` / `absent_all` / `regex_all` / `contains_and_absent` / `deferred`). Verify locally: `python evals/run_codex_miss_eval.py` reports `summary["all_pass"] == True` (counted over `accounted = total - deferred - pending`). Cases with `expected_status: "pending"` are baseline-excluded from `all_pass` accounting until their fix-forward ship lands and the row-flip closes the pending state.

2. **OR record `no_eval_case_reason`** in the fix-forward commit body documenting why the verdict can't be reduced to a deterministic case (e.g. natural-language regression that needs a Phase 3 codex judge; semantic equivalence; multi-step trajectory).

Bare prose-only verdicts without one of (1) or (2) are NON-COMPLIANT. The eval-case mandate is the operational form of "compounding learning across fires" that `karpathy-adapted.md` §5 describes — without replayable cases, the same class of miss can recur silently.

**REJECT verdicts**: still file an eval case if a state-flip is the fix (e.g. plan §2 row → RETIRE/REJECT-status); the case verifies the flip happened. If the REJECT is a no-ship outcome with no state to verify, record the reason in the wiki Run Log instead.

**APPROVE verdicts**: typically don't generate eval cases (no regression to capture). Exception: APPROVE that surfaces a methodology insight worth replaying (e.g. T2 dogfooding eval-runner pattern) — file as a positive-class case.

For Phase 2-4 of this rule (auto-add hook, codex judge, evolve_pass_rate_gate.py fallback), see `evals/README.md` "Future phases" + Task #88.

# Convergence Gate — Adopt Only When Pattern Has Converged

# TIER-3 port provenance only: ported from Z:/claude/rules/convergence-gate.md HEAD. Parent CCC retains cycle-N audit evidence; claude-sota authority is the TIER-1/TIER-2 sources cited inside this rule.

## The rule
A new pattern, repo, SDK, or external primitive is ADOPT-NOW only when CONVERGENCE evidence is present. Convergence means the pattern exists independently in multiple authoritative places, not just one. Absent convergence, the default verdict is **REJECT-until-convergence** — not STUDY-PILOT, not "maybe later."

This gate stacks ABOVE the `.claude/skills/sota-research/SKILL.md` 5-row LOAD-BEARING bar (LOCAL — sota-research skill present at `.claude/skills/sota-research/SKILL.md`). It is stricter: the 5-row bar allows STUDY-PILOT on any `[UNKNOWN]`; the convergence gate does not — any missing convergence axis is a REJECT until that axis is satisfied OR the STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS predicate fires.

## Convergence axes (ALL three required for ADOPT-NOW)

| Axis | Threshold | Evidence shape |
|---|---|---|
| **Independent T1 sources** | ≥3 distinct orgs/repos/papers implementing the pattern | file:line cites in 3 different `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/` dirs OR 3 different official docs URLs — not 3 pages of the same source |
| **Named T2 practitioners** | ≥2 named practitioners publicly adopting | Recognizable engineers (Karpathy / Simon Willison / Boris Cherny / Anthropic staff / equivalents), with a dated artifact (tweet, blog post, commit, conf talk) citing THIS specific pattern — not generic category endorsement |
| **Stability** | ≥3 months from pattern emergence to evaluation date | first public commit/post ≥90d old; <90d = "new and shiny," re-evaluate after the burn-in period (UNLESS STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS applies — see §Axis 3 5-band table) |

Any axis failing = REJECT-until-convergence. Verdict line must name the specific missing axis + the exact evidence that would satisfy it.

## Terminology disambiguation

**Convergence-gate Axis 3** = **stability** (age + cpd; quantitative scalar measuring repo/pattern maturity). About: "is this pattern old enough to trust?"

**`.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` Subclaim-type discriminator** (UPSTREAM-CLAIM / OPERATIONAL-CLAIM / CATEGORY-CLAIM) = **claim-source classification** (qualitative shape; about what's being verified, not when). About: "which kind of evidence does this claim require?"

Same word "axis" appears in both rules referring to different concepts. Use full names ("Axis 3 stability" vs "subclaim-type discriminator") when ambiguity matters.

## How to apply

Before writing any ADOPT verdict in `reports/sota-*.md` or `reports/*-audit*.md`:

1. **List the 3 T1 sources explicitly**. If you cannot name 3 different `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/file:line` OR 3 different official docs URLs where the pattern appears independently, the axis fails.
2. **Name the 2 T2 practitioners explicitly with dated artifact**. "Karpathy endorses" without a tweet URL + date is not evidence; it's wishful.
3. **Check the stability clock**. First commit / first public post date — `mcp__github__get_file_contents` on first-commit metadata, or the earliest blog post / paper date. <90d = candidate for STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS only (see §Axis 3); else fail.
4. **If ANY axis fails**, write `REJECT-until-convergence` as the verdict, with a bullet list specifying exactly what evidence would close each missing axis.
5. **Only then** run the sota-research 5-row bar. The convergence gate is a precondition — passing it doesn't auto-ADOPT, but failing it auto-REJECTS.

## Evaluation methodology — concrete tools per axis

Server-family availability per `.claude/rules/research-protocol.md:52-69` Tool Routing (iter-2 port). Function-level support marked `use-if-available` where the iter-2 routing table didn't independently verify the specific call shape.

### Axis 1 — Independent T1 sources (≥3 distinct)

Parallel enumeration via complementary tools — each catches what the others miss:

| Tool family | Strength | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `mcp__github__search_code` / `mcp__github__get_file_contents` | Exact symbol / import-pattern matches across GitHub | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__octocode__githubSearchCode` | Cross-repo code search with stable query identifiers — substitute: `mcp__github__search_code` | REMOVED (2026-05-03 Round-5d; FM-04 D6 firm) |
| `mcp__deepwiki__read_wiki_structure` + `read_wiki_contents` | AI-generated structured wiki per repo — surfaces patterns not in README keywords | ACTIVE (use-if-available; structure/content calls returned 404 on some repos in prior cycles) |
| `mcp__deepwiki__ask_question` | Repo-level Q&A without reading every file | ACTIVE — verify-only, not sole evidence |
| `mcp__repomix__pack_remote_repository` + `grep_repomix_output` | Cross-file pattern scan inside one repo | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__context7__resolve-library-id` + `query-docs` | Official docs as T1 (when adopting a library) | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__exa__web_search_exa` (category:research paper) | Peer-reviewed papers + named-org blogs as T1 | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__firecrawl__firecrawl_scrape` / `firecrawl_search` | Live-scrape when target is JS-rendered or behind soft paywall | ACTIVE |

**Rule**: at least 3 of these must return positive evidence for the SAME pattern from 3 DIFFERENT orgs/repos/papers. Not 3 different tools on the same source.

### Axis 2 — Named T2 practitioners (≥2 with dated artifact)

| Tool family | Use | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `mcp__exa__web_search_exa` (category:people, full name + pattern) | Find blog posts / talks by named practitioners | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__perplexity__perplexity_search` (recency filter: month/year) | Fresh endorsements — recognizable practitioners mentioning the pattern | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__firecrawl__firecrawl_scrape` on social profiles | Verify a claimed tweet/post actually exists and is dated | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__github__get_me` / search-users / repo collaborator metadata | Employer / org affiliation signals | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__deepwiki__ask_question` on the candidate repo | "Who are the major users or contributors from named orgs?" — triangulate | ACTIVE — use-if-available |

**Mandatory step — WebFetch README + grep practitioners**: GraphQL/`mcp__github__` collaborator metadata is necessary but **not sufficient** for axis 2. Named-practitioner endorsements frequently live in README content (direct quotes, acknowledgments, case studies) rather than repo-collaborator metadata. Before scoring axis 2, ALWAYS:
1. `WebFetch` (or `mcp__firecrawl__firecrawl_scrape`) the README
2. Grep for named-practitioner tokens recognizable in the relevant ecosystem
3. Require URL + date on each citation per the base rule

**Bidirectional**: the README-grep step upgrades verdicts when collaborator-metadata missed a signal AND confirms-REJECT when README also shows zero. Without the README-grep step, canonical repos with direct practitioner endorsements can be scored [UNKNOWN] → REJECT incorrectly.

**Rule**: each claimed practitioner endorsement must have a URL + date. "X tweets about Y" without a link is [UNKNOWN], not T2.

### Axis 3 — Stability (≥3 months first-public-artifact age, scored across 5 bands)

| Tool family | Use | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `mcp__github__get_file_contents` / commit history | `createdAt` / first-commit on the repo — repo age | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__exa__web_search_exa` (date range) | Earliest blog post / paper mentioning the pattern | ACTIVE |
| `mcp__arxiv__search_papers` + `get_abstract` | Paper publication date for research-backed patterns | ACTIVE |

**Rule**: 90 days minimum from EARLIEST public artifact, not from the version you're looking at. A v2.0 release isn't stability evidence if v1.0 was 30 days ago.

**Supplementary signal — commits/day (cpd)**: raw age alone under-specifies stability. A 99-day-old repo shipping 15 commits/day is structurally different from a 99-day-old repo shipping 1 commit/week — the former is still in active iteration, not burn-in. Compute `cpd = total_commits / age_days` alongside age:

| cpd × age band | Classification | Implication |
|---|---|---|
| `cpd < 10` AND `age ≥ 90d` | Stable burn-in | Firm axis-3 PASS |
| `10 ≤ cpd ≤ 20` AND `90d ≤ age ≤ 180d` | Active iteration | Borderline axis-3 — PASS-with-caveat; downgrade to STUDY-PILOT or CONDITIONAL-ADOPT if axis-2 also PARTIAL |
| `cpd > 10` AND `age > 180d` | **Sustained active maintenance** | Firm axis-3 PASS — mature age proves stability AND velocity signals ongoing investment, NOT rewrite churn. Classic shape: high-velocity repos past burn-in (avoid misclassifying as fast-churn anti-pattern) |
| `cpd > 10` AND `age < 100d` | Fast-churn anti-pattern | Treat age-PASS as borderline, expect rewrites, re-audit after +90d |
| **STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS** (5th band, predicate-gated): `age ≥ 30d` AND axis-1 = official-org maintainership AND axis-2 = named-T2 endorsement OR maintainer-org IS T2-equivalent | **Firm axis-3 PASS — relaxed maturity gate** | When provenance is strong (org-level T1) AND a named-T2 OR org-equivalent endorsement exists, the launch-spike risk that motivates the strict 90-day gate is dominated by maintainer-org commitment. Examples of org-level T1 patterns: vercel-labs / anthropics / openai / getzep / langchain / microsoft / google / facebook-research / google-deepmind / openai-evals / equivalents. The LAUNCH-SPIKE band still applies for `unknown-org + age<90d + cpd>50` — protection holds for the truly anti-pattern cases (solo-org launch with non-permissive license = REJECT-UNTIL-CONVERGENCE unchanged) |

The bands are exhaustive — every candidate falls into exactly one. STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS is a predicate-gated relaxation, not a default fallback; verify all three predicate clauses (≥30d age, official-org maintainer, named-T2 or org-equivalent endorsement) before applying it.

### Fusion rule (Rider 4 discipline)

No single tool is authoritative. Running any ONE of the above in isolation produces narrow-grep false-negatives (single-tool signal ≠ convergence). The convergence check requires ≥3 distinct tool-types returning compatible evidence — otherwise treat the finding as "one-tool signal," which is not convergence.

Cost discipline: cheap-first. `mcp__github__` calls (cheap, rate-limited) before `mcp__firecrawl__firecrawl_agent` (multi-minute credits). DeepWiki `ask_question` before packing the whole repo with repomix. If 3 cheap signals converge, don't escalate to expensive ones; if they don't converge, ONE expensive confirmatory signal is worth more than three more cheap ones.

## Anti-pattern: retroactive convergence manufacturing

Do not construct convergence evidence after choosing the verdict. The sequence must be: (1) collect convergence evidence → (2) count axes → (3) verdict. Not (1) pick verdict → (2) find supporting cites.

If a candidate looks promising but fails convergence today, the right move is to **queue a re-audit date** (90 days out, or when a named T2 practitioner is spotted adopting it), not lower the gate.

## Anti-pattern: Row-2 fabrication-test FAIL

When running the 5-row bar's Row 2 (Numeric — measurable improvement), a README presenting **≥3 numeric performance claims without methodology citation** (no repro script link, no benchmark repo, no dataset name) is **auto-FAIL** — do not attempt live A/B to verify, just REJECT. This short-circuits the primary-source rule: vendor marketing numbers that aren't backed by a reproducible artifact are false-by-default until proven otherwise.

**Procedure**:
1. Count numeric claims in README that make improvement/speedup assertions
2. For each, check: does the README cite a reproducible artifact (benchmark script path, dataset name, tool+version)?
3. If ≥3 claims lack citations, Row-2 auto-FAIL → REJECT verdict bypasses expensive verification step
4. If 1-2 claims lack citations, run the live-verify anyway (lower signal-to-noise)
5. If all claims cite reproducible artifacts, proceed to full primary-source check

**Why auto-FAIL not just downgrade**: across multiple prior live A/B verifications of fabrication-pattern candidates, none survived verification. The prior probability of "≥3 unsourced claims yet verifiable" is empirically near-zero. Running the bench is budget-wasting ritual.

**Three-tier evidence-density ladder** (illustrative exemplars; "evidence-density gradient" = how much reproducible methodology sits behind the numeric claim):

- **Tier 0** (auto-FAIL): `tirth8205/code-review-graph` — ≥3 README numeric claims lack a live methodology artifact; claimed `evaluate/reports/summary.md` absent in verified checkout (numeric claims live in diagrams/tables/narrative) [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via `Z:/repos/deps/code-review-graph/README.md:32,68,91-104,120-123,146-159,183-194 @ HEAD 0919071a9ba353e604981059e99ee2ed98768092`].
- **Tier 2** (strong-PASS): `context-mode` — `BENCHMARK.md` with 21 fixtures + repro instructions [VERIFIED 2026-05-03 via `Z:/repos/deps/context-mode/BENCHMARK.md:1-16,36-49,59-72,140-172 @ HEAD e40102e534632b137d2b9425b5ddd318222d114e`].

## Even-shorter-path: upstream self-flags impostor-domain risk

If a README opens with a `[!CAUTION] Scam alert` block warning of impostor domains distributing malware, that's a STRONG axis-2 self-flag — the upstream itself is signaling the candidate exists in a high-volume scam-attractor space (typically a viral-young repo where bad actors create lookalike forks). Auto-REJECT axis-2 even before counting unsourced numeric claims.

**How to apply**: when scanning a README for axis-2 named-practitioner endorsements, also scan the FIRST 10 lines for `Scam alert` / `[!CAUTION]` blocks warning of impostor domains. If present, the candidate has been or currently is in a high-attention attractor space — REJECT-until-convergence even if other axes look strong (the self-flag indicates ongoing trust-volatility).

**Distinguishes from §Anti-pattern Row-2 fabrication-test FAIL**: that rule fires on candidate AUTHORS publishing unsourced claims (deception via omission). This rule fires on candidate AUTHORS warning about THIRD-PARTY impostors (deception attractor signal). Same auto-REJECT axis-2 verdict, different mechanism.

## Sibling-rule notes

- `.claude/rules/research-protocol.md` §Benchmark-first sub-rule cites this rule's Axis-3 5-band table as the convergence-gate for benchmark-derived adoption candidates. This port satisfies that forward-ref.
- `.claude/rules/evidence-policy.md` Marker Decay corollary: **Marker Decay is claim freshness at reuse time** ("[VERIFIED yesterday] may be [INFERRED] today if upstream changed"). **Convergence-gate Axis 3 stability is adoption maturity** ("repo / pattern is old enough to trust as ADOPT-NOW"). The two are conceptually adjacent — both touch on temporal aspects of evidence — but they are NOT substitutes:
  - Marker Decay: "is THIS marked-claim still valid?"
  - Axis 3 stability: "is THIS candidate mature enough to adopt?"
  - A claim can be [VERIFIED today] (Marker Decay PASS) about a young repo (Axis 3 FAIL).
- `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Subclaim-type discriminator is a different axis-shape (claim-source classification, see §Terminology disambiguation above).

# Contributing to context-mode

This project is licensed under the Elastic License 2.0 (ELv2) and moves forward with your support. Every issue, every PR, every idea matters.

Don't overthink it. Don't ask yourself "is my PR good enough?" or "is this issue too small?" -- just send it. A rough draft beats a perfect plan that never ships. If you found a bug, report it. If you have an idea, open an issue. If you wrote a fix, submit the PR.

That said, I'm a solo maintainer with limited time. The best way to help me help you: follow the templates, run the debug script (`bash scripts/ctx-debug.sh`), and write tests for your changes. The more context you give me, the faster I can review.

I genuinely love open source and I'm grateful to have you here. Don't hesitate to reach out -- whether it's a question, a suggestion, or just to say hi. Let's build this together.

---

This guide covers the local development workflow so you can test changes in a live Claude Code session before submitting a PR.

## Architecture Overview

context-mode uses a flat `src/` structure:

```
src/
  server.ts        → MCP server, tool handlers, auto-indexing
  store.ts         → FTS5 content store (index, search, chunking)
  executor.ts      → Polyglot code executor (11 languages)
  security.ts      → Permission enforcement (deny/allow rules)
  runtime.ts       → Runtime detection (Node, Bun, Python, etc.)
  db-base.ts       → SQLite base class (shared by store + session)
  truncate.ts      → Smart output truncation
  cli.ts           → CLI commands (setup, doctor)
  types.ts         → Shared type definitions
  session/
    db.ts          → SessionDB — persistent event storage
    extract.ts     → Event extractors for PostToolUse hook
    snapshot.ts    → Resume snapshot builder (priority tiers)
  adapters/
    types.ts       → HookAdapter interface, RoutingInstructionsConfig
    detect.ts      → Platform detection via env vars
    claude-code/   → Claude Code adapter (index.ts, hooks.ts, config.ts)
    qwen-code/     → Qwen Code adapter (extends Claude Code wire protocol)
    gemini-cli/    → Gemini CLI adapter
    opencode/      → OpenCode adapter
    codex/         → Codex CLI adapter
    vscode-copilot/ → VS Code Copilot adapter
  openclaw/
    workspace-router.ts → Workspace path resolution for Pi Agent sessions
  openclaw-plugin.ts   → OpenClaw gateway plugin entry (sync register)
hooks/               → Plain JS hooks (.mjs) — no build needed
configs/             → Per-platform install files (settings.json, mcp.json, CLAUDE.md, etc.)
```

`tsc` compiles `src/` → `build/`. `start.mjs` loads `server.bundle.mjs` (CI-built) if present, otherwise falls back to `build/server.js`.

> **Critical for local dev:** Delete `server.bundle.mjs` in your local clone or your `build/server.js` changes will never be loaded:
> ```bash
> rm server.bundle.mjs  # forces start.mjs to use build/server.js
> ```

### Session Continuity Architecture

Session events flow through a two-database system:

1. **SessionDB** (persistent, per-project): `~/.claude/context-mode/sessions/<hash>.db`
   - PostToolUse hook captures events in real-time
   - PreCompact hook builds resume snapshots
   - UserPromptSubmit hook captures user prompts

2. **ContentStore** (ephemeral, per-process): `/tmp/context-mode-<PID>.db`
   - FTS5 full-text search index for tool outputs
   - Auto-indexes session events file written by SessionStart hook
   - Dies when MCP server process exits

**Session restore flow** (compact/resume):
```
SessionStart hook → reads SessionDB → writes events as markdown file
                  → injects ~275 token directive (summary + search queries)
MCP server        → detects markdown file on next getStore() call
                  → auto-indexes into FTS5 → deletes file
LLM               → searches source:"session-events" for details on demand
```

Raw session events are **never injected into context**. Only a compact summary table + search queries are injected. The LLM searches for details via the existing `ctx_search()` MCP tool.

## Prerequisites

- [Claude Code CLI](https://docs.anthropic.com/en/docs/claude-code) installed
- Node.js 20+ or [Bun](https://bun.sh/) (recommended for speed)
- context-mode plugin installed via marketplace

## Local Development Setup

### 1. Clone and install

```bash
git clone https://github.com/mksglu/context-mode.git
cd context-mode
npm install
npm run build  # tsc compiles src/ → build/
```

### 2. Symlink the cache to your local clone

Claude Code's plugin system manages `~/.claude/plugins/installed_plugins.json` and **will revert manual edits on restart**. The reliable approach is to replace the cache directory with a symlink to your local clone.

First, find your cached version:

```bash
ls ~/.claude/plugins/cache/context-mode/context-mode/
# Example output: 0.9.23
```

Then replace it with a symlink:

```bash
# Back up the cache (use your actual version number)
mv ~/.claude/plugins/cache/context-mode/context-mode/0.9.23 \
   ~/.claude/plugins/cache/context-mode/context-mode/0.9.23.bak

# Symlink to your local clone
ln -s /path/to/your/clone/context-mode \
   ~/.claude/plugins/cache/context-mode/context-mode/0.9.23
```

Replace `/path/to/your/clone/context-mode` with your actual local path.

> **Why symlink?** The plugin system overwrites `installed_plugins.json` on every session start, reverting any manual path changes. A symlink lets the plugin system keep its managed path while the actual code resolves to your local clone.

> **Critical:** The symlink must point to the root of your clone (where `hooks/`, `build/`, and `src/` all live). Hooks registered in `hooks.json` use `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` which resolves to this directory.

### 3. Update PreToolUse hook in settings

The symlink in step 2 ensures `hooks.json` (which registers PostToolUse, PreCompact, SessionStart, and UserPromptSubmit) resolves to your local clone via the plugin system. You only need to override PreToolUse in `~/.claude/settings.json` since its broader matcher is needed for dev mode:

```json
{
  "hooks": {
    "PreToolUse": [
      {
        "matcher": "Bash|Read|Grep|WebFetch|Agent|mcp__plugin_context-mode_context-mode__ctx_execute|mcp__plugin_context-mode_context-mode__ctx_execute_file|mcp__plugin_context-mode_context-mode__ctx_batch_execute",
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "node /path/to/your/clone/context-mode/hooks/pretooluse.mjs"
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
```

Replace `/path/to/your/clone/context-mode` with your actual local path.

> **Important:** Do NOT add PostToolUse, PreCompact, SessionStart, or UserPromptSubmit to `settings.json` — they are already registered in `hooks.json` and the symlink makes them resolve to your local clone. Adding them to both causes double invocations, split session IDs, and SQLite locking errors.

### 4. Bump the version for verification

Change the version in your local clone to something recognizable:

```bash
# All 4 files must be updated:
# 1. package.json:              "version": "0.9.23-dev"
# 2. src/server.ts:             const VERSION = "0.9.23-dev";
# 3. .claude-plugin/plugin.json:     "version": "0.9.23-dev"
# 4. .claude-plugin/marketplace.json: "version": "0.9.23-dev"
```

Then rebuild:

```bash
npm run build
```

### 5. Kill cached MCP processes and restart

```bash
# Kill any running context-mode processes
pkill -f "context-mode.*start.mjs"

# Verify no processes remain
ps aux | grep context-mode | grep -v grep
# Should return nothing
```

Restart Claude Code (`/exit` then `claude`).

### 6. Verify local dev mode

Run `/context-mode:ctx-doctor` in Claude Code. You should see your dev version:

```
npm (MCP): WARN — local v0.9.23-dev, latest v0.9.23
```

The version warning is expected -- it confirms you're running from your local clone, not the cache.

### Restoring marketplace version

To switch back to the marketplace version:

```bash
# Remove symlink and restore backup
rm ~/.claude/plugins/cache/context-mode/context-mode/0.9.23
mv ~/.claude/plugins/cache/context-mode/context-mode/0.9.23.bak \
   ~/.claude/plugins/cache/context-mode/context-mode/0.9.23
```

Then revert hooks in `~/.claude/settings.json` and restart Claude Code.

## Development Workflow

### Build and test your changes

```bash
# TypeScript compilation
npm run build

# Run all tests (parallel via Vitest)
npm test

# Type checking only
npm run typecheck

# Watch mode
npm run test:watch
```

### What needs rebuild?

| Changed | Rebuild needed? | Why |
|---------|:-:|---|
| `hooks/*.mjs` | No | Plain JS, loaded fresh each invocation |
| `src/*.ts` | Yes | Compiles to `build/` (MCP server, executor, store) |
| `src/session/*.ts` | Yes | Compiles to `build/session/`, imported by hooks |
| `src/adapters/**/*.ts` | Yes | Compiles to `build/adapters/`, platform detection + hooks |
| `configs/*` | No | Static files, served directly |

After rebuilding, restart your Claude Code session. The MCP server reloads on session start.

> **Tip:** If you only changed hook files (`hooks/*.mjs`), just restart Claude Code — no rebuild needed. Hooks are plain JS loaded fresh on each invocation.

### Key files to know

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/server.ts` | MCP server, tool handlers, auto-indexing of session events |
| `src/store.ts` | FTS5 content store (index, search, chunking) |
| `src/executor.ts` | Polyglot code executor (JS, Python, Shell, etc.) |
| `src/session/db.ts` | SessionDB — persistent session event storage |
| `src/session/extract.ts` | Event extractors for PostToolUse hook |
| `src/adapters/detect.ts` | Platform detection (Claude Code, Gemini CLI, etc.) |
| `src/adapters/types.ts` | HookAdapter interface, shared adapter types |
| `hooks/sessionstart.mjs` | Session lifecycle (startup/compact/resume/clear) |
| `hooks/posttooluse.mjs` | Real-time event capture from tool calls |
| `hooks/precompact.mjs` | Resume snapshot builder (fires before compact) |
| `hooks/pretooluse.mjs` | Tool routing + context window protection |
| `hooks/session-helpers.mjs` | Shared utilities (stdin reader, session ID, DB paths) |

## TDD Workflow

We follow test-driven development. Every PR must include tests.

**We strongly recommend installing the context-mode-ops skill** — it includes TDD enforcement, issue triage, PR review, and release automation with parallel subagent orchestration:

```bash
npx skills add mksglu/context-mode --skill context-mode-ops
```

### Red-Green-Refactor

1. **Red** -- Write a failing test for the behavior you want
2. **Green** -- Write the minimum code to make it pass
3. **Refactor** -- Clean up while keeping tests green

### Test file organization

**Do NOT create new test files.** Add your tests to the existing file that covers the same domain. We maintain a small number of well-organized test files — one per adapter, one per core module. Creating a new file per feature or per PR leads to fragmentation that makes the suite harder to navigate and maintain.

| Domain | Test File |
|---|---|
| Adapters | `tests/adapters/<platform>.test.ts` |
| Client detection | `tests/adapters/detect.test.ts`, `tests/adapters/client-map.test.ts` |
| Search & FTS5 | `tests/core/search.test.ts` |
| Server & tools | `tests/core/server.test.ts` |
| CLI & bundle | `tests/core/cli.test.ts` |
| Routing | `tests/core/routing.test.ts` |
| Hook routing | `tests/hooks/core-routing.test.ts` |
| Hook formatting | `tests/hooks/formatters.test.ts` |
| Hook integration | `tests/hooks/integration.test.ts` |
| Cursor hooks | `tests/hooks/cursor-hooks.test.ts` |
| Gemini hooks | `tests/hooks/gemini-hooks.test.ts` |
| VS Code hooks | `tests/hooks/vscode-hooks.test.ts` |
| JetBrains hooks | `tests/hooks/jetbrains-hooks.test.ts` |
| Kiro hooks | `tests/hooks/kiro-hooks.test.ts` |
| Session DB | `tests/session/session-db.test.ts` |
| Session extract | `tests/session/session-extract.test.ts` |
| Session snapshot | `tests/session/session-snapshot.test.ts` |
| Session continuity | `tests/session/continuity.test.ts` |
| Session pipeline | `tests/session/session-pipeline.test.ts` |
| Executor | `tests/executor.test.ts` |
| Store/Search | `tests/store.test.ts` |
| Security | `tests/security.test.ts` |
| OpenClaw plugin | `tests/plugins/openclaw.test.ts` |

If your change doesn't fit any existing file, discuss with the maintainer before creating a new one.

### Output quality matters

When your change affects tool output (ctx_execute, ctx_search, ctx_fetch_and_index, etc.), always compare before and after:

1. Run the same prompt **before** your change (on `main`)
2. Run it **again** with your change
3. Include both outputs in your PR

## Testing the OpenClaw Adapter

The OpenClaw adapter has its own test suite and installation workflow.

### Running tests

```bash
npx vitest run tests/plugins/openclaw.test.ts tests/adapters/openclaw.test.ts
```

These tests run without a live OpenClaw instance — they mock the plugin API.

### Local OpenClaw testing

To test against a running OpenClaw gateway:

1. Install the plugin:
   ```bash
   npm run install:openclaw
   # Or with a custom state directory:
   npm run install:openclaw -- /path/to/openclaw-state
   ```
   The script picks up `$OPENCLAW_STATE_DIR` from your environment (default: `/openclaw`). It handles building, native dependency rebuild, extension registration, and gateway restart in one step.

2. Open a Pi Agent session and verify hooks fire by checking the debug log output.

See [`docs/adapters/openclaw.md`](docs/adapters/openclaw.md) for hook registration details and known upstream issues.

## Submitting a Bug Report

When filing a bug, **always include your prompt**. The exact message you sent to the agent is critical for reproduction. Without it, we can't debug the issue.

Required information:
- Debug script output: `bash scripts/ctx-debug.sh` (collects OS, runtimes, configs, hooks, SQLite diagnostics)
- The prompt that triggered the bug
- Full error output (expand with `Ctrl+O` in Claude Code)
- Steps to reproduce

## Submitting a Pull Request

1. Fork the repository
2. Create a feature branch from `next`
3. Follow the local development setup above
4. Write tests first (TDD)
5. Run `npm test` and `npm run typecheck`
6. Test in a live Claude Code session
7. Compare output quality before/after
8. Open a PR using the template

| Task | Command |
|---|---|

| Upgrade plugin | `/context-mode:ctx-upgrade` |
| View session stats | `/context-mode:ctx-stats` |
| Purge knowledge base | `/context-mode:ctx-purge` |
| Run diagnostics | `bash scripts/ctx-debug.sh` |
| See background steps | `Ctrl+O` |
| Kill cached server | `pkill -f "context-mode.*start.mjs"` |
| Rebuild after changes | `npm run build` |
| Run all tests | `npm test` |
| Watch mode | `npm run test:watch` |
